RBI Banking and Economy 
 
RBI Hikes Repo Rate by 25 bps to 5.50% as Inflation Outlook Worsens; Stance Shifts to 
Calibrated Tightening 
Key Points 
• The Monetary Policy Committee  unanimously raised the policy repo rate  by 25 basis 
points to 5.50%, shifting its stance to calibrated tightening.  
• The standing deposit facility rate is now 5.25%, while the marginal standing facility 
rate and Bank Rate are 5.75%. 
• The committee said rate cuts are off the table in the near term, and the next move can 
only be a rate hike or a pause. 
• Global conditions have deteriorated, with the West Asia conflict driving volatile crude prices, 
sharply higher expected inflation, and tighter monetary policy by major central banks.  
• Global growth is projected to slow in 2026, with energy and food prices pushing inflation 
up. 
• Trade uncertainty, rising bond yields in advanced economies, a stronger dollar, and 
doubts about AI stock valuations are the main downside risks. 
• Domestic growth remains resilient, with real GDP expanding 7.8% in Q1 of 2026-27.  
• The growth forecast for 2026-27 was raised by 40 basis points  to 7.1%, with Q2 
projected at 7.2%. 
• Monsoon deficiency and El Niño conditions could weigh on the upcoming rabi season 
and rural demand. 
• Inflation is rising across food, fuel, and core components, prompting the rate hike.  
• CPI inflation rose to 4.8% in August 2026 from 4.5% in July, and core inflation climbed 
to 4.2%. 
• CPI inflation for 2026-27 is projected at 5.2%, with core inflation at 4.4%. 
• The share of items with inflation above 4% rose to about 37%, signalling broadening 
price pressures. 
• Liquidity is in surplus, but credit growth remains robust and financial sector parameters are 
sound.  
• System liquidity averaged a daily surplus of ₹5.9 lakh crore since the August meeting. 
• Scheduled commercial banks and NBFCs continue to report strong capital, liquidity, 
asset quality, and profitability. 
• The external sector shows a modest current account deficit, but the merchandise trade 
deficit widened.  
• The merchandise trade deficit rose to US$ 58.7 billion in July-August 2026 from US$ 
55.1 billion a year earlier. 
• Net FDI inflows reached US$ 13.8 billion in April-August 2026, while foreign portfolio 
investors pulled out US$ 10.3 billion. 
• Foreign exchange reserves stand at about 11 months of import cover. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
4 
 
• The central bank announced two new measures.  
• Account aggregators  will be allowed to interoperate, and SEBI-regulated 
depositories will include deposit account information in consolidated account 
statements, both by December 31, 2026. 
• A Technical Consultative Committee for Financial Markets will be formed to engage 
market participants on policy and operational matters. 
 
Fiscal Deficit Widens to ₹7.10 Trillion in April -August as Capex Rises 18% and Subsidies 
Climb 25% 
Key Points 
• The Centre's fiscal deficit widened to ₹7.10 trillion in April-August 2026-27, a near 19% 
jump from a year earlier.  
• The deficit reached 42% of the Budget Estimate, compared with 38% in the same period 
last year. 
• Strong disinvestment proceeds helped limit the widening so far this year. 
• Higher capital expenditure and rising subsidy spending drove the widening, while revenue 
growth remained muted.  
• Capital expenditure rose 18% year-on-year to ₹5.10 trillion, meeting about 42% of the 
planned FY27 capex. 
• Major subsidies rose nearly 25% to ₹1.87 trillion, led by a near 21% jump in fertiliser 
subsidies. 
• The subsidy bill reached 46% of the full -year Budget Estimate, up from 39% a year 
earlier. 
• Revenue trends were mixed across tax heads.  
• Net tax collections  after devolution to states rose 3.5% to ₹8.38 trillion, while gross 
tax collections grew 6.5% to ₹14.32 trillion. 
• Excise duty  collections fell nearly 23% to ₹86,687 crore , following a cut in special 
additional excise duty on petrol and diesel in late March. 
• Customs duty collections rose 28% to ₹1.07 trillion, aided by duty hikes on gold and 
silver imports. 
• Non-debt capital receipts more than doubled, supporting fiscal space.  
• Non-debt capital receipts rose to ₹75,239 crore from ₹31,970 crore a year earlier. 
• ICRA estimates the deficit could overshoot the FY27 Budget Estimate by ₹1.3-1.4 trillion.  
• The government has projected the FY27 fiscal deficit  at ₹16.96 trillion , or 4.3% of 
GDP. 
• ICRA expects a shortfall in income tax and excise duty collections, partly offset by 
customs duty gains. 
• Expenditure savings of ₹1.6-1.7 trillion recorded in FY26 could absorb the overshoot. 
• Bank of Baroda's chief economist expects the fiscal deficit ratio to stay around 4.5-4.6% of 
GDP this year.  
• The projection assumes some slippage in non-tax revenue as oil marketing companies 
may not generate profit. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
5 
 
 
Net GST Revenue Rises 18.1% to ₹1.77 Trillion in September, Fastest Growth in Six Months 
Key Points 
• Net GST receipts of the Centre and states, after refunds, rose 18.1% year-on-year to ₹1.77 
trillion in September 2026.  
• This was the fastest growth in six months, according to provisional data released by the 
Finance Ministry. 
• Net GST revenue growth was about 14% in the July-September quarter, compared with 
about 7% in the June quarter. 
• Net collections grew 15.8% in July and 8.3% in August. 
• Gross GST collections rose 14.7% to ₹2.04 trillion in September, crossing the ₹2 trillion 
mark for the third time in six months.  
• Domestic GST revenue rose 10.1% to ₹1.38 trillion. 
• Earlier peaks were ₹2.43 trillion in April and ₹2.11 trillion in July. 
• IGST on imports is growing faster than domestic GST collections, supported by import-linked 
revenue.  
• IGST on imports contributed ₹65,525 crore in September before refunds, growing nearly 
26%, or about a third of gross GST collections. 
• Net GST from imports rose 30.8% to ₹52,028 crore, while net domestic GST rose 13.5% 
to ₹1.24 trillion. 
• Experts linked the import surge partly to the West Asia war , which raised world 
commodity prices and weakened the rupee against the dollar. 
• Higher capital expenditure in mobile-phone manufacturing, power and defence also 
boosted imports of capital goods and inputs. 
• Refunds were slightly lower year-on-year.  
• Total refunds stood at ₹27,001 crore in September, 3% lower than a year earlier. 
• Export-related refunds rose 10.2% to ₹13,497 crore, while domestic refunds fell 13.5% 
to ₹13,504 crore. 
• Customs duty revenue of the Centre rose 28% in April-August to ₹1.06 trillion, against a 
budgeted growth of only 5% for FY27. 
• Across April-September, gross GST revenue rose 11.6% to ₹12.46 trillion, and net GST 
revenue rose 10.4% to ₹10.66 trillion. 
• Experts see the next phase of GST 2.0 focusing on process rationalisation, such as easier 
credit claims, refunds and dispute resolution. 
 
RBI Extends Benchmark Issuance Strategy to Seven More States and J&K from October -
December 2026 
Key Points 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
6 
 
• The Reserve Bank of India extended its Benchmark Issuance Strategy (BIS) framework 
to seven more states and the Union Territory of Jammu and Kashmir , taking the total 
coverage to 26 states and UTs.  
• The new entrants are Assam, Goa, Haryana, Mizoram, Nagaland, Tripura and 
Jammu and Kashmir, effective from the October-December quarter of FY27. 
• The framework aims to enhance transparency and give investors greater clarity. 
• The BIS was introduced on a pilot basis in Q1 FY27 for nine states, and nine more states 
and Delhi were brought under it from Q2.  
• The pilot states were Andhra Pradesh , Bihar, Chhattisgarh, Kerala, Madhya 
Pradesh, Maharashtra, Rajasthan, Telangana and Uttar Pradesh. 
• State governments and UTs  are expected to borrow ₹3.61 trillion  through market 
borrowings during October-December 2026.  
• The actual borrowing amount and participating states will be announced two to three 
days before each auction. 
• Announcements depend on government requirements, Centre's approval under the 
Constitution and prevailing market conditions. 
• The RBI will try to conduct auctions in a non-disruptive manner and spread borrowings 
evenly across the quarter.  
• The central bank retains flexibility to change auction dates and amounts in consultation 
with state governments and UTs. 
 
UPI Volumes Rise 22.6% to 24.07 Billion in September 2026; Daily Average Hits a Record 
802 Million 
Key Points 
• UPI transaction volumes grew 22.6% year-on-year to 24.07 billion in September 2026, from 
19.63 billion in September 2025.  
• Transaction value rose 18% to ₹29.37 trillion over the same period. 
• Month-on-month, volumes fell 1.8% from 24.51 billion  in August, mainly because 
September has 30 days against 31 in August. 
• UPI recorded its highest average daily volume of 802 million transactions in September.  
• The average daily value across all transactions was ₹97,913 crore. 
• Daily transactions stood at 757 million in June, a 30-day month. 
• Month-on-month dips of low single digits are common because of differences in the number 
of days in a month.  
• Of the past 24 months, 10 saw declines of 1.0% to 2.3% on this basis. 
• Declines of 5.2% to 6.7%  were seen in February 2025  and February 2026 , and in 
November 2024, which was linked to Diwali falling in October that year. 
• The rise in September value was driven by festival spending, as Ganesh Chaturthi fell in 
mid-September.  
• From April to August, daily value stayed around ₹96,000 crore, as new payments were 
mostly small. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
7 
 
• A merchant discount rate (MDR) will apply to select UPI payments from October 15, 2026, 
ending six years of zero-charge UPI transactions.  
• Peer-to-peer (P2P) transactions will remain free, as will payments to peer-to-merchant 
(P2PM) vendors, defined as small vendors receiving up to ₹1 lakh a month. 
• Peer-to-merchant transactions above ₹2,000 will attract a 0.40% fee, capped at ₹300. 
 
India's Forex Reserves Fall $18.3 Billion to $747.6 Billion, Biggest Weekly Decline on 
Record 
Key Points 
• India's foreign exchange reserves declined by $18.3 billion to $747.6 billion in the week 
ended September 25, 2026, the largest weekly fall on record.  
• The reserves had hit an all-time high of $785.7 billion in the week ended September 4, 
2026. 
• Reserves have fallen for three straight weeks, losing about $38.2 billion in total. 
• The RBI has been intervening in the forex market through spot and forward transactions to 
curb excessive volatility in the rupee.  
• The rupee depreciated 0.5% to 96.31 per dollar on the day of the report. 
• Pressures came from higher crude oil prices, with Brent crude crossing $100 a barrel, 
and a rise in US 10-year Treasury yields to 5.34%, their highest since 2002. 
• The breakdown of the latest weekly decline shows the largest fall in foreign currency assets 
(FCAs).  
• FCAs, the largest component, fell $15.6 billion to $615.4 billion. 
• Gold reserves declined $2.6 billion to $108.7 billion. 
• Special drawing rights (SDRs)  fell $97 million  to $18.6 billion , and the reserve 
position with the IMF fell $86 million to $4.8 billion. 
• The previous week's decline was $14.9 billion, the sharpest since November 2024. 
• Earlier, reserves had fallen to $666.9 billion in the week ended June 26, before recovering 
on the back of the FCNR(B) swap window , which mobilised $143.6 billion in inflows until 
September 18. 
• The RBI has also used dollar-rupee sell/buy swaps, which help replenish reserves while 
absorbing excess rupee liquidity from the banking system. 
 
Gross FDI Hits 15-Year High of $30.66 Billion in Q1 FY27; Net FDI at Five-Year Peak 
Key Points 
• Gross foreign direct investment (FDI)  reached $30.66 billion  in Q1 of 2026 -27, the 
strongest quarter in 15 years, surpassing the previous peak in Q2 of 2020-21.  
• Repatriation by foreign investors, through dividends, royalties and sale of equity , 
took out about 44% of gross FDI. 
• Foreign exchange equal to about 31% of gross FDI moved out as Indian companies 
invested abroad. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
8 
 
• Net FDI, calculated as gross inflows minus repatriation and outward investment, stood at a 
five-year high of $7.84 billion.  
• Net FDI was about 25% of gross FDI, and rose 50% from $5.24 billion a year earlier, 
against a 13% rise in gross inflows. 
• Gross FDI reached a record $96.99 billion in FY26.  
• RBI Deputy Governor Poonam Gupta has said gross FDI could cross $100 billion in 
FY27. 
• Net FDI has lagged gross inflows over the longer term.  
• Net FDI was over half of gross inflows in 28 of 44 quarters between 2011 and 2022, but 
has not crossed that mark in the 16 quarters since July-September 2022. 
• Net FDI averaged 54% of gross inflows over the first 44 quarters, compared with 12% 
over the last 16. 
• Negative net FDI, where repatriation and outward investment exceed gross inflows, has 
become more frequent.  
• It occurred only once in the first 44 quarters, during the Covid period, but has happened 
four times since July-September 2022. 
• The latest instance, in October-December 2025, was the deepest. 
• Repatriation has risen sharply, averaging 57% of gross inflows in the last 16 quarters, 
compared with 28% earlier.  
• RBI Governor Sanjay Malhotra described the rise as a sign of a mature market, while 
economist Biswajit Dhar warned that it could signal investors behaving like short -term 
speculators. 
• Equity inflows reached a six -year high of about $20 billion in the June quarter, with Japan 
overtaking Singapore as the top source.  
• MUFG Bank  bought a 20% stake in Shriram Finance  in April, the largest foreign 
investment ever in an Indian financial company. 
• Sumitomo Mitsui Banking Corporation became the largest shareholder in Yes Bank 
with a 24.22% stake. 
• Services now account for about half of equity inflows, with banking, insurance and other 
services taking 35.5%. 
 
India's Services PMI Rises to 55.2 in September, a Three-Month High on Stronger Demand 
Key Points 
• The HSBC India Services PMI  rose to 55.2 in September 2026  from 54.1 in August, 
signalling faster expansion in services activity.  
• Business activity growth reached a three-month high, as improving sales performance 
lifted the index. 
• The reading stayed above the 50 mark that separates expansion from contraction, 
marking the 62nd consecutive month in expansion territory. 
• New orders  rose at their fastest pace in three months, supported by both domestic and 
international demand.  


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
9 
 
• Demand from digital companies, food, insurance, loans, software, transportation, 
and tours and travel drove the expansion. 
• Finance and insurance  and consumer services  recorded the highest expansion 
among the tracked sub-sectors. 
• Input-cost pressures eased to a 10-month low during the month. 
 
RBI Removes Paytm Payments Bank from Schedule of Recognised Scheduled Banks After 
Licence Cancellation 
Key Points 
• The Reserve Bank of India (RBI)  has excluded Paytm Payments Bank Limited (PPBL)  
from the Second Schedule to the RBI Act, 1934 , removing it from the list of recognised 
scheduled banks.  
• A notification was published on July 31 and in the Gazette of India in September. 
• The RBI announced the step in a statement on Wednesday. 
• The RBI had cancelled PPBL's banking licence  in April 2026  for non -compliance with 
norms, saying the bank's affairs were conducted in a manner detrimental to the interest of its 
depositors. 
• The Delhi High Court later ordered that PPBL be wound up. 
• PPBL faced regulatory action over several years.  
• In March 2022 , it was barred from onboarding new customers due to material 
supervisory concerns, and was asked to appoint an IT audit firm for a comprehensive 
system audit. 
• On January 31, 2024  and February 16, 2024 , further restrictions barred deposits, 
credits and top-ups in existing accounts, prepaid instruments and wallets. 
• PPBL is an associate of the fintech firm Paytm, promoted by Vijay Shekhar Sharma. 
 
Apple launches Apple Pay in India with Axis Bank-issued credit cards 
• US-based technology company Apple Inc. launched Apple Pay in India in September 2026, 
initially supporting eligible Axis Bank-issued Visa and Mastercard credit cards. 
• Transactions can be authenticated through Face ID, Touch ID or a device passcode at 
contactless payment terminals. 
• Apple Pay uses an encrypted device account number and tokenisation technology to secure 
transactions. 
• The service's initial rollout covers categories such as food and beverages, travel, fashion and 
lifestyle. 
• The initial Indian offering is card-based and does not support UPI or RuPay cards. 
 
Bank of India launches programmable CBDC with auto-issuance 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
10 
 
• Bank of India (BoI) launched a user -level programmable Central Bank Digital Currency 
(CBDC) feature with auto-issuance at the Global FinTech Fest 2026 in Mumbai. 
• Developed by Montran India, the feature allows users to schedule recurring transfers of 
Digital Rupee (e₹) to beneficiaries' CBDC wallets at fixed amounts and frequencies. 
• Users can also specify restrictions on how recipients may spend the funds, supporting 
allowances, education expenses and other purpose-specific recurring payments. 
• The feature complies with RBI and NPCI guidelines. The RBI launched its wholesale Digital 
Rupee pilot on 1 November 2022 and retail Digital Rupee pilot on 1 December 2022. 
 
Small Savings Scheme interest rates unchanged for 14th consecutive quarter 
• The Department of Economic Affairs (DEA) under the Ministry of Finance kept Small Savings 
Scheme (SSS) interest rates unchanged for Q3 of FY 2026 –27, covering October 1 to 
December 31, 2026. 
• The rates remain the same as those applicable during Q2 FY 2026 –27 (July–September 
2026). 
• This marks the 14th consecutive quarter of unchanged interest rates, with the last revision 
made in Q4 FY 2023–24. 
• Small Savings Schemes are government -backed instruments designed to encourage 
household savings and provide secure investment options. 
• The schemes are managed by the DEA and administered through post offices and 
designated banks, with interest rates reviewed quarterly since 2016. 
 
Ministry of Finance Projects India’s GDP Growth at 7.3% in Q2 FY27 
• The Ministry of Finance projected India’s real GDP growth at 7.3% in Q2 FY27 (July –
September 2026) in its Monthly Economic Review for September 2026. 
• The projection follows 7.8% GDP growth in Q1 FY27 (April–June 2026) and is based on the 
government’s nowcasting model. 
• Consumption and investment remain key growth drivers, with electricity and fuel 
consumption, bank credit, capital goods production and infrastructure activity indicating 
continued momentum. 
• The services sector remained strong, supported by new business and employment, while 
automobile sales indicated steady rural and urban demand. 
• Total exports were nearly USD 400 billion during the first five months of FY27. The Ministry 
stated that overall exports could approach USD 1 trillion for the full financial year if the pace 
continues. 
 
RBI Grants Core Investment Company Registration to Indiabulls Limited 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
11 
 
• In October 2026, the Reserve Bank of India granted a Certificate of Registration (CoR) to 
Indiabulls Limited as a Core Investment Company (CIC). 
• A CIC is a category of NBFC that primarily holds investments in and provides funding to 
group companies. 
• As described in the supplied report, a CIC generally has assets of at least ₹100 crore and 
holds at least 90% of its net assets in group-company investments or loans, including at least 
60% in equity investments of group companies. 
• The registration brings Indiabulls Limited under the RBI's regulatory framework for CICs. 
• Indiabulls Limited, formerly Yaari Digital Integrated Services Limited, was established in 2000 
and is headquartered in Gurugram, Haryana. Its CEO is Divyesh B. Shah. 
 
RBI Announces Special Forex Facility for Oil Marketing Companies and Tightens 
Derivative Rules 
In October 2026, the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) introduced a designated foreign exchange 
window to meet the daily US dollar requirements of three public-sector oil marketing companies: 
Indian Oil Corporation (IOC), Hindustan Petroleum Corporation (HPCL) a nd Bharat Petroleum 
Corporation (BPCL). 
The special forex facility became operational on 12 October 2026. 
Forex Derivatives: The RBI prohibited authorised dealers from allowing users to rebook cancelled 
foreign exchange derivative contracts involving the Indian rupee, covering both deliverable and 
non-deliverable contracts. 
The RBI also reduced the applicable threshold from USD 100 million to USD 5 million across all 
authorised dealers. 
Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR): The RBI increased the minimum daily maintenance requirement for 
scheduled banks from 90% to 99% of the prescribed CRR, effective from the reporting fortnight 
beginning 16 October 2026. 
 
RBI's FERR rule requires 20% cash reserve on forex derivatives above $2 million 
Key Points 
Under the Foreign Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR)  framework, banks must maintain a cash 
reserve with the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee value of specified forex derivative transactions 
exceeding $2 million.  
The Foreign Exchange Dealers' Association of India (Fedai), the industry body for authorised 
dealers, is taking up the issue with the RBI and seeking clarity on how the rules will be 
implemented. 
The FERR rule follows the RBI's October 10 measures to curb speculative activity and excessive 
trading in rupee-linked forex derivatives, amid elevated crude oil prices and capital outflows.  
The measures include capping currency derivatives positions from $100 million to $5 million. 
Corporates are restricted from rebooking cancelled forward contracts. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
12 
 
The measures aim to discourage speculative positions and excessive turnover, including 
transactions booked and then cancelled or rolled over, rather than genuine hedging against trade 
exposures. 
The RBI has introduced a dedicated dollar supply window  for the three state -run oil -
marketing companies, routing their dollar requirements through the central bank instead of the 
spot market.  
The window does not lower the country's overall oil import bill. 
A State Bank of India (SBI)  report estimates that the reserve requirement, along with the other 
measures, could drain more than ₹1 trillion from the banking system over a longer period.  
The report estimates FERR alone could require around ₹80,000 crore in reserves against fresh 
contracts on a rolling basis. 
The estimate assumes monthly notional merchant forward sales stay at the FY27 average of 
$46.94 billion so far, that 10% of notional value falls below the $2 million threshold, and that 
the exchange rate is ₹95 per dollar. 
 
RBI raises daily CRR maintenance to 99% after 25 bps repo hike to 5.50% 
Key Points 
The RBI raised the minimum daily maintenance requirement for the cash reserve ratio (CRR) 
from 90% to 99% of the prescribed requirement, effective from the fortnight beginning October 
16, 2026.  
The move follows the RBI's decision to raise the policy repo rate by 25 basis points to 5.50%, 
its first increase in two and a half years. 
Banks must maintain at least 99% of the prescribed CRR on each day of the reporting fortnight, 
while their average daily CRR balance over the fortnight must be no less than the prescribed 3%. 
The RBI announced an open market operation (OMO)  sale of government securities worth 
₹25,000 crore on October 13 to absorb surplus liquidity.  
The auction uses the multiple-price method, with six government securities maturing between 
2030 and 2034 on offer. 
The RBI will decide the amount of each security sold and can accept bids for less than the notified 
amount or reject bids. 
In September, the RBI sold ₹1 trillion of government securities through OMOs in three tranches. 
The liquidity surplus, measured by funds banks parked in the liquidity adjustment facility  
window, stood at ₹3.88 trillion as on October 8.  
The measures aim to bring the weighted average call rate (WACR) , the operating target of 
monetary policy, closer to the repo rate of 5.50%. 
The WACR stood at 5.31% on Friday, while the standing deposit facility (SDF) rate  of 5.25% 
sets the floor and the marginal standing facility (MSF) rate of 5.75% sets the ceiling. 
Since the August monetary policy review, the WACR has traded on average 14 basis points 
below the policy repo rate. 
RBI Governor Sanjay Malhotra  said the RBI would use an appropriate mix of liquidity 
management tools to align the WACR with the repo rate. 
Banks parked ₹18,170 crore in the RBI's 10-day variable rate reverse repo (VRRR) auction.  
The three-day VRRR auction drew nearly ₹1.4 trillion against a notified amount of ₹1.5 trillion. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
13 
 
The RBI plans to conduct a three-day VRRR auction worth ₹2 trillion on Monday. 
The CRR requirement was 99% in July 2013, then lowered to 95% in September 2013 and to 
90% in April 2016. 
CRR is the share of bank deposits that banks must hold as cash balances with the RBI, on which 
they earn no interest. 
 
SME body seeks 90-day EDF filing relief for small service exporters 
Key Points 
The RBI has been asked by the India SME Forum  to suspend recurring Export Declaration 
Form (EDF) filings for micro and small service exporters for 90 days while a simpler compliance 
mechanism is finalised.  
The requirement falls under the Foreign Exchange Management (Export and Import of Goods 
and Services) Regulations, 2026. 
The request was made in a letter dated October 7. 
Under the RBI Governor Sanjay Malhotra 's clarification at the monetary policy press meet, 
individuals making transactions of a personal nature, including small services to overseas clients, 
do not have to report them, irrespective of the amount.  
Exporters can use self-declarations instead of invoices for transactions of up to Rs 10 lakh per 
bill. 
Recurring EDF filing requires exporters to maintain invoice-level records, including customer 
and country details, currency and transaction value, contract information and service 
classification.  
Exporters must also reconcile receipts and follow up with authorised dealer banks. 
The final rules include consolidated filing options , a payment-receipt filing route for non -
software services and simplified closure for smaller invoices. 
The India SME Forum has sought a clear exemption or a simplified annual compliance route 
for exporters below an appropriate annual export turnover or transaction-value threshold.  
It has proposed bank-led, automated reporting , with exporters contacted only when data is 
missing or discrepancies arise. 
For transactions that cannot be reconciled automatically, it has proposed consolidated periodic 
or annual self-declarations. 
The India SME Forum has also called for uniform procedures across authorised dealer banks, 
including standardised documentation, capped charges, time -bound closure and an escalation 
mechanism.  
It has asked that legitimate export receipts are not delayed or rejected solely because of initial 
EDF filing or reconciliation difficulties, subject to KYC and anti-money laundering checks. 
The India SME Forum  notes that goods and e -commerce exporters under the Export Data 
Processing and Monitoring System (EDPMS)  face similar delays, such as manual 
reconciliation, payment-platform deductions and purpose-code errors.  
It had commented on the draft FEMA regulations on July 31, 2025. 
It has sought an urgent implementation review involving the RBI, the finance ministry, trade 
authorities, authorised dealer banks, payment providers and exporter representatives. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
14 
 
 
RBI Excludes Paytm Payments Bank from Scheduled Banks List 
In October 2026, the RBI excluded Paytm Payments Bank Limited (PPBL) from the list of 
scheduled banks under Section 42(6)(b) of the RBI Act, 1934. 
The decision followed the cancellation of PPBL’s banking licence on 24 April 2026, due to 
regulatory non-compliance and conduct considered detrimental to depositors’ interests. 
In July 2026, the Delhi High Court ordered the bank’s winding up under the Banking Regulation 
Act, 1949, read with the Companies Act, 2013. 
Official Liquidator: Girikumar M. Nair, former Chief General Manager of the State Bank of India 
(SBI), was appointed as the Official Liquidator. 
Under the RBI Act, 1934, a scheduled bank must maintain paid-up capital and reserves of at least 
₹5 lakh and satisfy other statutory conditions. 
 
Wholesale inflation hits series high of 11.39% in September on broad rise 
Key Points 
WPI-based inflation rose to 11.39% in September from 9.92% in August, its highest level in 
the 2022 -23 series , driven by a broad -based rise in prices of food, fuel and manufactured 
goods.  
The all-commodities index rose to 112.5 in September from 110.8 in August. 
The data was released on Wednesday by the Ministry of Commerce and Industry. 
The goods output producer price index (PPI)  also rose to 112.5 in September, taking PPI 
inflation to 11.39% from 9.81% in August. 
Fuel and power inflation rose to 25.85% in September from 22.93% in August.  
Crude petroleum and natural gas inflation surged to 57.32% from 34.41%. 
Coal and lignite inflation  rose to 9.31% from 1.57%, and mineral oil inflation  moved up to 
40.02% from 38.48%. 
Electricity inflation remained negative at 0.43%, though the contraction eased from 1.73% in 
August. 
Fuel and power inflation had moderated between May and August, when crude petroleum and 
natural gas inflation eased from 61.51% in May. 
Primary articles inflation rose to 8.67% in September from 7.76% in August, the highest in the 
new series.  
Food inflation jumped to a 21-month high of 8.14% from 5.67%. 
Minerals inflation rose to 9.51% from 8.24%, while non-food inflation moderated to 10.28% 
from 14.79%. 
Manufactured products , which carry a weight of 63.1%, rose to 9.76%, the highest in the 
current 2022-23 series.  
Prices hardened within textiles, wearing apparel , chemicals, basic metals  and electrical 
equipment. 
Inflation for computer, electronic and optical products  rose to 4% after several months of 
contraction.

Core WPI, which excludes volatile food and fuel items, rose to 9.8% in September from 8.1% in

Centre approves sale of 60.72% stake in IDBI Bank to Fairfax for ₹52,878 crore 
Key Points 
The Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA) , chaired by PM Narendra Modi , 
approved on Wednesday the sale of a 60.72% stake in IDBI Bank , jointly held by the 
Government of India  and LIC, to Fairfax Financial Holdings  of Toronto for ₹52,878 crore  
(around $5.56 billion).  
Once completed, it will be the largest FDI in India's financial sector. 
Prem Watsa is the founder and chief executive officer of Fairfax Financial Holdings. 
The government will sell 30.48% of its 45.48% stake, leaving it with a residual 15%.  
LIC will sell 30.24% of its 49.24% holding, leaving it with 19%. 
An empowered group of ministers , headed by Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman , has 
been delegated powers to sell the government's residual 15% stake through the open market 
under the standard operating procedure. 
The transaction includes an open offer to minority shareholders, giving them an exit option.  
Fairfax must reduce its stake to 26% to meet a regulatory requirement, through a 15-year glide 
path to be worked out with the RBI. 
LIC will reduce its stake from 19% to 15% over two years. 
Fairfax holds a 40% stake in CSB Bank through Fairfax India Holdings Corporation and must 
either exit it or merge it with IDBI Bank within two years.  
Under RBI norms, an entity cannot own and operate two separate banks. 
The government has raised ₹55,757 crore through disinvestment this financial year, against a 
combined Budget target of ₹80,000 crore for disinvestment and asset monetisation. 
Of five parties that submitted expressions of interest, four qualified, and two submitted techno-
financial bids as qualified bidders.  
Emirates NBD was also reported to be in the race. 
Initial bids invited in FY26 were rejected for falling short of the reserve price of ₹94.42 per share. 
Fresh bids were invited in FY27, and the winning bid of ₹81 per share was above the average 
valuation of ₹75.99 per share. 
The winning bidder must present its credentials to the RBI for a fit-and-proper assessment and 
submit a roadmap for complying with the prescribed regulatory conditions before completion. 
The bank was set up as a Development Financial Institution in 1964, converted into a banking 
company in 2004, and is now transitioning to private control. 
IDBI Bank shares closed at ₹89.85 on the NSE on Wednesday, up 8.3% from the previous close.

India’s Forex Reserves Decline by $51.1 Billion in Four Weeks 
India’s foreign exchange reserves fell by $12.95 billion to $734.6 billion in the week ended October 
2, 2026, marking the fourth consecutive weekly decline. 
Reserves have declined by $51.1 billion from their record high of $785.7 billion recorded in the 
week ended September 4, 2026, amid RBI intervention to support the rupee and valuation losses. 
Foreign Currency Assets (FCA), the largest component of forex reserves, fell by $10.7 billion to 
$604.7 billion, while gold reserves declined by $2.3 billion to $106.4 billion. 
A concessional foreign exchange swap window mobilised $143.6 billion in foreign currency 
inflows by September 18, 2026, including $132.98 billion through Foreign Currency Non-Resident 
(Bank) [FCNR(B)] deposits. The window closed on August 31, 2026. 
Despite the recent decline, India’s reserves provide approximately 11 months of import cover and 
are equivalent to 94.4% of the country’s external debt. 
 
Securitisation Volume Hits Record ₹1.47 Trillion in H1 FY27: ICRA 
According to ICRA, India's securitisation volume rose 42% year-on-year to a record ₹1.47 trillion 
in the first half of FY 2026–27 (H1 FY27), compared with ₹1.04 trillion in H1 FY26. 
The growth was driven by a sharp rise in gold loan securitisation and a recovery in vehicle loan 
transactions. Gold loan securitisation volumes increased nearly 1.5 times over the previous year. 
In Q2 FY27, securitisation volumes reached ₹86,000 crore, supported by gold loans, vehicle loans 
and steady activity in the MSME and business loan segments. 
Vehicle loans accounted for around one-third of total securitisation volumes in H1 FY27. Mortgage 
loans, small business loans and micro-loans each contributed around 12–15%. 
ICRA has projected securitisation volumes of ₹2.6–2.7 trillion for FY27, potentially setting another 
annual record. Both Pass -Through Certificates (PTCs) and Direct Assignment (DA) routes 
accounted for roughly half of the volumes.

The National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI) has deferred the implementation of the 
Merchant Discount Rate (MDR) on select Person-to-Merchant (P2M) UPI transactions until after 
Diwali 2026. The new implementation date will be notified separately. 
The levy was originally scheduled to take effect on October 15, 2026. The deferment aims to 
provide relief to merchants during the festive season, while the proposed fee structure remains 
unchanged. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
20 
 
MDR is a fee paid by merchants to the bank and payment service providers processing a digital 
transaction. The proposed charges apply to eligible UPI merchant transactions above ₹2,000, at 
0.40% capped at ₹300; certain categories, including bill payments, fuel and education, will attract 
a flat fee of ₹5. 
In September 2026, UPI recorded 24.07 billion transactions worth ₹29.37 trillion, representing 
year-on-year growth of 22.6% in volume and 18% in value. 
NPCI operates UPI, India's real -time digital payment system. The deferment follows concerns 
raised by merchant groups over the proposed charges.

costs. 
The government had planned to raise the amount through 91-day, 182-day and 364-day T-bills. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
21 
 
The benchmark 10 -year government bond yield settled at 7.44 per cent, its highest since 
November 4, 2022, up from 7.39 per cent. 
This is the RBI's first bid rejection since June, when it cancelled 182 -day and 364-day auctions. 
At the end of March, it had rejected bids across all three maturities. 
Yields have risen sharply over the past five weeks. Between September 9 and October 14, cut -
off yields rose by 64 basis points (bps) on 91 -day bills, 83 bps on 182 -day bills and 69 bps on 
364-day bills. 
At the previous week's auction, the government raised funds at 5.73 per cent (91 -day), 6.29 per 
cent (182-day) and 6.49 per cent (364-day). 
The spread between the 364 -day T-bill yield and the repo rate widened to 99 bps, its highest in 
four years. 
The RBI has turned hawkish. It raised the repo rate to 5.50 per cent this month and shifted to a 
stance of calibrated tightening, leaving the door open for further hikes. Bets on a 50 bps hike in 
December are growing. 
Crude oil prices, with Brent around $112 per barrel, and rising US yields are adding pressure on 
government bonds. 
Liquidity has tightened after a long period of surplus. Net liquidity was a surplus of about ₹48,303 
crore on Tuesday, and the weighted average call rate (WACR), the operating target of monetary 
policy, settled at 5.64 per cent, close to the marginal standing facility (MSF) rate. 
The RBI conducted a ₹1 trillion variable rate repo (VRR) auction on Monday, its first in three 
months, and the overnight VRR on Wednesday drew bids of ₹93,241 crore against a notified 
₹50,000 crore. 
The RBI had been absorbing liquidity from August 6 and had raised the daily cash reserve 
requirement from October 16. 
 
Lenders flag early repayment stress in India's fast-growing gold loan book, with borrowers 
taking fresh loans to clear overdue debt 
Key Points 
Bajaj Finance and Piramal Finance have flagged early signs of stress in gold loans, as lenders 
expand their portfolios quickly against pledged jewellery. 
Between 15 and 23 per cent of gross gold loan disbursements across the industry in 2025 -26 
went to borrowers more than 90 days overdue on at least one other loan, according to Piramal 
Finance's MD and CEO Jairam Sridharan. 
He put the value of these disbursements at about ₹4.7 trillion and called the figure "shockingly 
high." 
Piramal Finance says such borrowers made up less than 1 per cent of its own gold loan 
disbursements in October, and it does not lend to borrowers more than 90 days past due. 
Lenders and analysts warn that gold collateral may protect them less than it appears. If borrowers 
pledge gold to repay overdue loans elsewhere, without improving their repayment capacity, their 
debt burden grows even though the jewellery is still held as security. 
Bajaj Finance, with a gold loan portfolio of about ₹24,000 crore, reported a rise in Stage 2 assets 
(31-90 days overdue) in the July-September quarter. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
22 
 
It attributed ₹1,047 crore of the increase to short -tenor gold loan schemes introduced after the 
RBI's revised gold loan guidelines. It stopped offering those schemes in August and says the 
deterioration is largely transient. 
Stage 2 and Stage 3 assets rose by ₹1,601 crore during the quarter, and about ₹550 crore had 
already moved back to Stage 1. 
Its vice-chairman Rajeev Jain said the company prefers early intervention over auctioning pledged 
gold. 
Gold loans have become India's second-largest retail lending category after mortgages, at nearly 
28 per cent of the non -housing retail loan book, according to Macquarie Capital's Suresh 
Ganapathy. 
Outstanding gold loans from banks rose 83 per cent year on year to about ₹5.6 trillion by August 
31, 2026. 
Outstanding gold loans from NBFCs rose 69 per cent to about ₹3.6 trillion over the same period, 
according to RBI data. 
Analysts say the risk is greater if gold prices fall sharply while defaults rise, which could force a 
wave of auctions and weaken recoveries. Auctions also face operational constraints, including 
rules on where they can be held. 
India Ratings' Jinay Gala notes that borrowers in smaller cities may depend on gold as their main 
financial asset, while urban borrowers with larger working -capital loans may have different 
repayment capacity.

Key Points 
India's trade deficit widened to $30.21 billion in September from $26.86 billion in August,

Merchandise exports rose 23 per cent year on year to a four-month high of $44.40 billion. 
Merchandise imports rose 8 per cent year on year to $74.61 billion, their slowest pace of growth 
in six months.

along with meat, dairy and poultry products, handicrafts and some cereals, according to 
Commerce Secretary Rajesh Agrawal.

Petroleum product exports rose 32 per cent to $6.22 billion. 
Organic and inorganic chemical exports rose just over 24 per cent to $2.94 billion. 
On the import side, crude oil imports rose about 31 per cent to $18.34 billion, and electronic goods 
imports jumped about 52 per cent to $14.86 billion. Gold imports nearly halved to $1.8 billion, 
which Agrawal attributed to the customs duty hike on the me tal in May. Fertiliser imports fell 22 
per cent to $1.84 billion. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
23 
 
Services exports were estimated at $36.35 billion in September, nearly flat year on year, while 
services imports rose 9 per cent to $19.44 billion. This gives an estimated services trade surplus 
of $16.91 billion. The RBI will release the final services data later this month. 
For the April to September period, goods exports reached $259.89 billion, up nearly 19 per cent. 
Imports rose 16 per cent to $437.53 billion, and the merchandise trade deficit stood at $177.64 
billion. 
The US remained India's largest export market, with exports rising about 48 per cent to $8.03 
billion in September. 
China remained the largest import source, with imports rising about 32 per cent to $14.86 billion. 
Agrawal expects merchandise exports to reach around $500 billion by the end of FY27. 
Exports made up 14 per cent of GDP in the first quarter of FY27, recovering to the level of FY23, 
after falling to 11.3 per cent in FY26. 
 
India's current account deficit narrows to $5.2 billion in August, while the balance of 
payments posts a $45.4 billion surplus 
Key Points 
India's current account deficit (CAD) narrowed to $5.2 billion in August from $6.1 billion a year 
earlier, according to RBI data. A lower merchandise trade deficit and higher net services receipts 
offset a wider deficit in the income account. 
The merchandise trade deficit narrowed to $26.6 billion from $27.6 billion, as merchandise exports 
rose to $44.7 billion from $35.1 billion and imports rose to $71.3 billion from $62.7 billion. 
Net services receipts rose to $16.5 billion from $15.6 billion, with services exports at $35.5 billion 
and services imports at $18.9 billion. 
Net transfers, which include remittances from Indians working abroad, rose to $12.8 billion from 
$12.2 billion. 
The net income account deficit widened to $8 billion from $6.3 billion. 
The balance of payments (BoP) recorded a surplus of $45.4 billion in August, against a deficit of 
$5.6 billion a year earlier. The capital account inflow far exceeded the current account deficit. 
Net banking capital recorded inflows of $64.7 billion, compared with outflows of $4.7 billion in 
August 2025. 
Net NRI deposits stood at $83 billion, against $1 billion a year earlier. 
Net foreign direct investment (FDI) inflow was $3.3 billion, and portfolio investment inflow was 
$1.7 billion. 
External commercial borrowings (ECBs) recorded a net outflow of $22.4 billion, sharper than the 
$0.1 billion outflow a year earlier. 
For April to August, the CAD widened to $18.3 billion from $12.8 billion, as the merchandise trade 
deficit grew to $146 billion from $124.7 billion. Net services receipts rose to $85.8 billion and net 
transfers rose to $66.8 billion. The overall BoP surplu s for the period was $58 billion, against a 
deficit of $0.8 billion a year earlier. 
ICRA expects the CAD to widen to $13-15 billion in Q2 FY27, from $4.2 billion in Q1 FY27. 
 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
24 
 
India's unemployment rate holds at 5% in September, with women's joblessness edging 
up to 5.2% 
Key Points 
India's unemployment rate (UR) stayed at 5 per cent in September, according to the Periodic 
Labour Force Survey (PLFS) monthly bulletin released by the National Statistics Office (NSO). 
Both the labour force participation rate (LFPR) and the worker populat ion ratio (WPR) dipped 
marginally. 
The rural unemployment rate rose to 4.2 per cent from 4.1 per cent in August. 
The urban unemployment rate rose to 6.9 per cent from 6.8 per cent, using the current weekly 
status (CWS) measure. 
Joblessness rose more sharply among women, to 5.2 per cent from 5.1 per cent. 
Urban women's unemployment rose to 9.2 per cent from 8.9 per cent, while rural women's rate 
held steady at 3.9 per cent. 
The female WPR fell to 32.8 per cent from 33 per cent, while the male WPR rose to 73.6 per cent 
from 73.5 per cent. 
For men, the overall unemployment rate stayed at 4.9 per cent. Rural men's rate rose to 4.4 per 
cent from 4.3 per cent, while urban men's rate fell to 6 per cent from 6.1 per cent. 
The LFPR fell moderately to 55.5 per cent from 55.6 per cent. It rose in rural areas to 50.7 per 
cent but fell in urban areas to 58 per cent. 
The WPR dipped to 52.7 per cent from 52.8 per cent. It fell in rural areas to 55.5 per cent but rose 
in urban areas to 47.2 per cent. 
Under the CWS approach, a person is unemployed if they did not work at all in the seven -day 
reference week but looked for work or were available for work for at least one hour on any day in 
that week. 
The PLFS sampling method was revamped in January 2025 to estimate key employment and 
unemployment indicators monthly for rural and urban areas, at the all-India level. 
 
RBI clarifies banks must hold a 20% forex risk reserve in their CRR accounts, over and 
above the mandatory CRR balance 
Key Points 
The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) clarified through FAQs that banks must keep the 20 per cent 
foreign exchange risk reserve (FERR) in their cash reserve ratio (CRR) accounts, over and above 
the mandatory CRR balance. Banks must keep records so the RBI can ve rify compliance during 
audits. 
The clarification formalises an arrangement banks reached among themselves on Monday 
evening, when the new rules took effect. 
The FERR equals 20 per cent of the rupee value of the notional amount of eligible foreign 
exchange derivative contracts. 
The reserve applies to specified rupee -linked contracts where users buy foreign currency to 
hedge current account transactions and the notional value exceeds $2 million. Banks must also 
hold it against eligible contracts that hedge their own currency risks on assets and liabilities. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
25 
 
Banks must maintain the reserve daily for each eligible transaction and keep it until one day after 
the derivative contract ends. 
Transactions from Monday onwards are covered, whether they are fresh contracts or rollovers. 
The RBI has also restricted the rebooking of cancelled derivative contracts. 
The reserve pushes up hedging costs because the funds held with the RBI earn no interest. 
Forward premiums have risen sharply as banks and customers unwind positions and adjust to 
the rules. 
Some banks advise customers to book shorter two - to three-month forward contracts instead of 
hedging for longer, in the hope that the measures will be eased once markets stabilise. However, 
there is no certainty the RBI will withdraw them. 
Banks that raised FCNR(B) deposits are assessing how to hedge the interest component of their 
dollar exposure. Some have hedged through forwards and other derivatives, while others have 
left part of the exposure unhedged, depending on their view of the rupee. 
 
Supreme Court upholds Bombay HC ruling quashing Yes Bank's ₹8,415 crore AT1 bond 
write-off 
Key Points 
The Supreme Court upheld the Bombay High Court's decision to set aside Yes Bank's ₹8,415 
crore Additional Tier 1 (AT1) bond write-off of March 2020. It held that the administrator appointed 
by the RBI acted beyond his authority. 
A bench of Justices Dipankar Datta and Augustine George Masih dismissed appeals filed by Yes 
Bank, the RBI and the Union government. 
The write-off was announced on March 14, 2020. The Bombay HC had quashed it in its January 
20, 2023 judgment. 
The court said the RBI should have written down the bonds once the point of non -viability was 
triggered, but the administrator, not the RBI, did so. It held that the administrator could not 
exercise a power that belonged to the RBI. 
The write-down had to happen before the reconstruction scheme took effect on March 13, 2020. 
Once the scheme came into force, the administrator could not extinguish the bonds, because the 
government had removed the write-down provision from the final scheme. 
A general clause preserving existing contracts did not give the administrator specific authority to 
write down the bonds. 
The legality of the action was to be judged on the facts as they stood on March 14, 2020, not on 
Yes Bank's later financial position. 
The court said the RBI remained a "mere bystander" while Yes Bank's Common Equity Tier 1 
(CET1) ratio fell to 0.6 per cent. It also said Yes Bank sold high-risk AT1 instruments to individual 
investors, despite RBI rules limiting their distribution to specified categories. 
Yes Bank shares fell 14.4 per cent on the day of the ruling, their steepest single -day fall since 
July 23, 2020. 
Coupon payments on the bonds are unaffected and must be paid according to the bond 
information memoranda. Each party bears its own costs.

Key Points

which is the highest growth in five years.

which calls for a matching change in monetary policy. 
 
RBI tightens operational risk capital norms for banks, effective April 1, 2027 
Key Points 
The RBI has revised norms for calculating banks' operational risk capital, effective April 1, 2027. 
The business indicator now has three components: the Interest, Lease and Dividend Component 
(ILDC), the Services Component (SC) and the Financial Component (FC). 
Outsourcing fees for non-financial services (such as IT, logistics and HR) are excluded from the 
Services Component. 
Interest rate derivative assets must be measured at fair value, not notional value, and only positive 
fair values are included. 
The loss component for the internal loss multiplier (ILM) is 15 times the average annual 
operational risk losses, net of recoveries. 
Banks must generally use 10 years of high-quality loss data; first-time ILM users may use at least 
5 years.

Travel, the largest component, rose 0.7% to $1.63 billion, while overseas education fell 39.9% to 
$191.8 million. 
Remittances rose 4.5% year -on-year to $12.55 billion in April -August FY27, led by equity and 
debt investment, which doubled to $1.73 billion. 
The LRS was introduced in 2004 and now allows resident individuals to remit up to $250,000 per 
financial year; the initial limit was $25,000.

RBI revises leverage ratio norms for banks, effective April 1, 2027 
Key Points 
The RBI has revised the leverage ratio framework for banks, effective April 1, 2027, to align it with 
the Basel Committee's 2017 standard. 
The minimum leverage ratio is 4% for D-SIBs and 3.5% for other banks. 
Indian branches of G-SIBs must maintain 3.5% plus the applicable leverage ratio buffer. 
Banks must generally use gross accounting values for on -balance-sheet exposures, with no 
offsetting of assets and liabilities or reduction through collateral and guarantees, except where 
specifically permitted. 
Capital and exposure measures are calculated at the end of each quarter, and banks must 
disclose Basel III leverage ratios quarterly on standalone and consolidated bases.

drivers of credit growth.

December 2025. 
Vehicle loans, the largest retail segment, slowed, and microfinance/SHG loans declined. 
Secured lending is about three-fourths of NBFC loans and advances and was the main driver of 
credit growth.

of Artificial Intelligence), which proposed seven sutras to guide AI adoption in the financial sector.

RBI's new forex derivative rules make FCNR(B) hedging costlier for banks 
Key Points 
Under the RBI's Foreign Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) framework, banks must set aside a 
reserve equal to 20% of the rupee value of the notional amount of eligible forex derivative 
contracts. 
It applies to rupee -linked contracts where foreign currency is bought to hedge current account 
transactions and the notional value exceeds $2 million. 
Transactions from October 12, including fresh contracts and rollovers, are covered, and the 
rebooking of cancelled contracts is restricted. 
The reserve earns no interest, which raises hedging costs. 
The 12-month dollar-rupee forward premium rose to 4.38% from 3.36% at the end of September, 
an increase of 102 basis points. 
Banks mobilised $133 billion of FCNR(B) deposits through the RBI's special swap window, 
against an estimate of $75-100 billion, so the RBI closed the window on August 31, a month early. 
The swap window covers only the principal, so banks must hedge the interest payments 
themselves. 
 
Satellite night-time lights and Google Trends can help estimate state growth: RBI Bulletin 
study 
Key Points 
An RBI Bulletin study by RBI staffers, titled "Nowcasting State -Level Economic Activity in India 
with Night -Time Light and Google Trends", uses satellite night -time lights and Google search 
trends to estimate quarterly gross state value added (GSVA) growth. 
Official GSVA data is released annually, with a lag of 12-18 months. 
The authors' views are their own and not the RBI's. 
The model uses NASA's Black Marble night-time light data and Google Trends indices across 25 
search categories, along with past GSVA growth. 
Estimation error (RMSE) fell by about 62% for Gujarat, 54% for Karnataka and 38% for 
Maharashtra, compared with a benchmark model based on past GSVA growth. 
The correlation between night -time light growth and GSVA growth was highest in Maharashtra 
(0.90), followed by Karnataka (0.79) and Gujarat (0.66). 
The framework is meant to supplement official statistics, not replace them.

Inflows into Non -Resident Indian (NRI) deposit schemes surged 21.6 times to $121.94 billion 
during April–August FY27, compared to $5.6 billion in the corresponding period of the previous

Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
31

NSE Plans Pilot App and Wider Collection Network to Revive Electronic Gold Receipts 
Key Points 
• The National Stock Exchange plans to boost electronic gold receipts (EGRs), a segment 
that has seen weak trading since its May launch.  
• Trading volumes were minimal in September, ranging between ₹1 crore and ₹2 crore 
with around 50 trades a day. 
• A pilot app allowing investors to convert physical gold into dematerialised EGR holdings 
within two hours is expected to go live on October 15. 
• The exchange plans to widen the network of authorised collection centres, which will test 
the purity and weight of gold submitted for conversion.  
• Centres will first be rolled out across another 50 to 100 locations after a one- or two-
week pilot. 
• The number of authorised centres is planned to exceed 1,000 over the next year, 
eventually reaching a few thousand across cities. 
• NSE will offer six EGR denominations, from 10 milligrams to 1 kilogram, in two purities 
to improve margins and attract retail participation.  
• Smaller denominations are expected to encourage wider retail participation. 
• EGRs are dematerialised securities backed by physical gold stored in vaults accredited 
by the market regulator and held electronically through depositories.  
• Each EGR is tradable on the exchange and can be converted back into physical delivery. 
• Agreements with refiners, assayers, vault managers  and depositories have been 
completed, but operational readiness remains a constraint.  


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
35 
 
• Industry players said back-office and front-office systems are still being built and tested, 
so clients cannot yet trade EGRs despite their availability on exchanges. 
• An additional 3 per cent GST  on EGRs is seen as a possible drag on demand, and 
industry players have urged regulators to address potential double taxation. 
• Industry experts expect brokers may later offer systematic investment plans in EGRs once 
adoption gains traction. 
 
SEBI Revamps Document Verification System to Curb Fake Letters and Notices 
Key Points 
• The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI)  revamped its Document Number 
Verification System for verifying physical letters and notices.  
• The subject matter of a letter is now a mandatory field for verification. 
• Recipients of SEBI letters, including notices and show-cause notices, or anyone acting on 
their behalf, can check a letter's origin.  
• Verification requires the outward number, the issuing official's name, the letter date, 
the subject matter and the recipient's name. 
• The check confirms only the letter's origin and does not verify the contents of the letter. 
• The changes are meant to strengthen the credibility of authentication  and reduce the 
circulation of fake letters.

SEBI's Closing Auction Session Review Draws Over 20,000 Responses; Traders Resist 
VWAP Shift 
Key Points 
• SEBI received more than 20,000 comments  on its consultation paper on the closing 
auction session (CAS), with traders and market participants largely favouring continuation 
of the 30-minute CTS-VWAP method.  
• The CAS was introduced for stocks with derivatives contracts from August 3, 2026 , 
and was followed by a sharp fall in futures and options (F&O) trading volumes. 
• SEBI is expected to examine the feedback before finalising the revised CAS framework. 
• SEBI proposed two options for determining expiry-day settlement prices.  
• Option 1 is a blended VWAP combining trades in the last 30 minutes of continuous 
trading session (CTS) and the 10-minute CAS. 
• Option 2 is continuation of the CTS-only VWAP for at least one year before considering 
a transition to the blended method. 
• Feedback from traders, brokers and other participants favoured the second option, 
arguing the existing method is familiar, transparent and operationally well understood. 
• The Futures Industry Association (FIA)  strongly opposed immediate adoption of the 
blended VWAP and backed CTS-VWAP as the interim method.  
• FIA argued that variable weights for CTS and CAS would only be known after the auction, 
making it hard to track settlement prices. 
• FIA recommended that any move to blended VWAP be conditional on a clear liquidity 
trigger and further consultation. 
• On market timings, FIA and several traders backed extending continuous trading in CAS 
stocks to 3:30 pm, followed by the auction, with derivatives trading until 3:45 pm. 
• FIA opposed SEBI's proposal to stop disseminating the indicative index value (IIV) during 
CAS, arguing it aids transparency and surveillance. 
• Other proposals in the consultation include:  
• Allowing unexecuted Iceberg orders to enter CAS as fully displayed normal limit orders. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
37 
 
• Shortening the transition between continuous trading and CAS to up to one minute. 
• Prohibiting cancellation of limit orders placed beyond a 1% range of the reference price 
during CAS, while permitting only price-improving modifications. 
• SEBI officials had earlier noted that the VWAP method was prone to manipulation. 
 
IRDAI Distribution Overhaul Could Cut Insurance Brokers' Revenue by 70%, Says IBAI 
Key Points 
• The Insurance Brokers Association of India (IBAI)  has sought a deeper assessment of 
the Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (IRDAI)  proposal to 
overhaul insurance distribution norms.  
• The industry estimates a 60-70% revenue impact and about 1 million jobs at risk. 
• IBAI has requested an extension of the feedback deadline to the end of December. 
• The broking industry has asked for an assessment of the impact on revenues, jobs and 
policyholder servicing, along with a glide path for brokers similar to the transition proposed 
for insurers. 
• Proposed changes to commissions and expenses of management (EoM)  could make a 
large part of broking business economically unviable.  
• Corporate broking , where 60-70% of jobs could be affected, is seen as particularly 
vulnerable. 
• Brokers warned that excess commissions could be disguised as marketing fees or other 
payments, encouraging unethical practices. 
• Brokers cited IRDAI data showing that premiums sourced through brokers  in general 
insurance grew 37% between FY23 and FY25, while commissions paid to brokers rose 
173%.  
• The average commission rate increased from 8.5% to 17% over the same period. 
• The industry argues that lower commissions will not automatically reduce policyholder costs, 
as distributors bear expenses for acquisition, renewals, servicing and claims assistance.  
• The impact could be sharpest for low-ticket policies, where commissions may not cover 
customer servicing costs. 
• Brokers warned that consolidation could reduce customer access, especially for smaller 
customers and businesses, and cut the number of intermediaries who help policyholders 
with claims.  
• They also argued that brokers and agents should not be treated alike, given their 
different responsibilities and regulatory obligations. 
 
Mutual Fund Industry's Average AUM Rises 5% to ₹87.2 Trillion in Q2 FY27 Despite Equity 
Volatility 
Key Points 
• Mutual fund houses' quarterly average assets under management (QAAUM) rose nearly 
5% to ₹87.2 trillion in Q2 FY27, from ₹83.1 trillion in Q1.  


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
38 
 
• The growth was the highest in three quarters, according to data released by the 
Association of Mutual Funds in India (AMFI). 
• The rise came despite mark -to-market losses in equity schemes from a market 
correction, especially in the large-cap segment. 
• Strong inflows across categories supported the growth.  
• In July and August, investors put a net ₹54,000 crore into active equity schemes. 
• Net inflows across all categories reached ₹2.8 trillion over the same two months, with 
debt funds receiving the largest share. 
• Hybrid funds  and passive funds  each attracted nearly ₹10,000 crore  in the two 
months. 
• Experts said debt fund inflows were led by short-term institutional investments , while 
active equity inflows were driven by systematic investment plan (SIP) contributions.  
• SIPs account for the bulk of equity inflows, and since collections happen throughout the 
month, they add to the average AUM. 
• Corporate liquidity into short-term debt funds also supported the average AUM. 
• The large-cap correction in the last two weeks of September had limited impact on the 
average AUM. 
• AMFI's data for September is yet to be released. 
 
SEBI Introduces Mandatory Credit Risk-o-Meter for Debt Securities and Eases Private Debt 
Norms 
Key Points 
• SEBI introduced a mandatory Credit Risk -o-Meter for debt securities  to help investors 
assess credit risk through colour-coded visualisation.  
• The framework applies to listed and proposed issuances  of non-convertible 
securities, commercial papers, securitised debt instruments, security receipts and 
structured debt. 
• The meter maps credit ratings from AAA to D across six risk levels. 
• SEBI eased norms for privately placed debt, raising the maximum number of International 
Securities Identification Numbers (ISINs) maturing in a financial year to 17.  
• Of these, 12 are for plain vanilla debt , a limit that can rise for issuers with large 
maturities, and 5 are for structured-type debt. 
• An additional six ISINs are allowed for Section 54EC capital gains tax bonds. 
• NSE cautioned investors against trading certain international ETFs  that are trading at 
substantial premiums to their net asset value (NAV).  
• Overseas investment limits  available to mutual funds have been fully utilised, 
restricting the creation of fresh ETF units. 
• Investors buying at steep premiums could face sharp price declines, particularly if 
overseas limits are raised or ETF price-band rules change from April 1, 2027. 
• Investors are advised to check the latest NAVs on the exchange, the AMFI website or 
their trading platforms before placing orders.

Sebi extends relaxation on revising IPO fresh issue size till March 31, 2027 
Key Points 
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Sebi)  has extended its relaxation allowing 
companies to revise the size of fresh issues until March 31, 2027, according to a communication 
sent to the Association of Investment Bankers of India (AIBI).  
The earlier relaxation, introduced in view of the West Asia war, expired on September 30. 
The communication was sent by email to AIBI on October 12. 
Under the relaxation, IPO-bound companies can increase or decrease the size of their fresh 
issue by up to 50% without filing fresh draft offer documents with the regulator.  
The lead manager can file an updated offer document reflecting the revised issue size, provided 
the issue's main objective remains unchanged. 
Merchant bankers must justify the revision and ensure all relevant changes are disclosed to the 
public through an addendum, keeping the offer documents compliant with applicable regulations. 
The relaxation applies to IPOs opening on or before March 31, 2027. 
Primary market activity was high in September, when more than 34 companies raised around 
₹39,380 crore, accounting for 42% of the total funds raised in FY27.  
As of September, 78 companies had raised around ₹94,276 crore in FY27. 
The NSE debut in September, described as the largest IPO of this financial year , contributed 
to the high figures.

SEBI Introduces Mandatory Colour-Coded Credit Risk-o-Meter for Debt Securities 
In October 2026, the Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) issued a circular introducing 
a colour-coded Credit Risk-o-Meter to help investors understand the credit risk associated with 
debt securities. 
Effective Date: The framework will come into force 45 days after issuance, around 21 –22 
November 2026, as stated in the source. 
Mandatory Disclosure: The Credit Risk -o-Meter must appear in offer documents, abridged 
prospectuses, private placement memoranda, issuer advertisements and the websites and 
mobile platforms of Online Bond Platform Providers (OBPPs). 
Applicability: The framework covers Non -Convertible Securities (NCS), Commercial Papers 
(CPs), Securitised Debt Instruments (SDIs), Security Receipts (SRs), and Structured 
Debt/Market-Linked Debentures (MLDs). 
Existing credit ratings ranging from AAA to D will be mapped to six risk levels, represented through 
colours, from “Lowest Credit Risk” to “High to Very High Risk of Default”.

Artha Bharat moves two funds from GIFT City to Abu Dhabi Global Market, citing tax 
uncertainty and compliance burden 
Key Points 
Artha Bharat Investment Managers IFSC, a fund management entity (FME) in GIFT City 
managing over $800 million in assets, is relocating two of its funds to Abu Dhabi Global Market 
(ADGM). 
The first is the master fund of the $112 million Artha Global Multiplier Fund, which invests mainly 
in the US consumer theme and is open only to non-resident Indians (NRIs). 
The second is the recently launched Artha Bharat Absolute Return Fund, an arbitrage fund 
investing in Indian securities. 
The firm is setting up a new fund in ADGM, and investors in the current funds may move to it. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
46 
 
The firm cites tax uncertainty in GIFT City. It says its FME was denied the tax deduction under 
Section 80LA of the Income Tax Act, 1961, and it is contesting the decision. 
It also flags withholding tax on payments to overseas service providers such as law firms and 
research vendors, even though the money comes from foreign investors and is invested abroad. 
The CEO says leading fund jurisdictions generally tax at the investor level, while India has several 
categories with differentiated taxation. 
The firm says the move is not an exit from GIFT City. Its other funds will still be managed from 
GIFT City and will provide risk management and technology support to entities in ADGM, 
Mauritius and Singapore. 
Minimum ticket size is a further concern. Funds in GIFT City require $150,000, which is high 
compared with the $250,000 annual Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS) limit for Indian 
investors. In ADGM the minimum is $55,000, which the firm says will widen its investor base. 
One fund has not attracted any investors in 15 months because it cannot accept money from 
resident Indians. Mixing resident and non-resident investors would make it lose its specified fund 
status and be taxed at the fund level. 
A law firm partner says GIFT City funds must keep meeting prescribed tests on investor base, 
holding concentration, resident participation and corpus to stay within the safe harbour, and 
breaches can carry tax consequences. He says Abu Dhabi offers a simpler framework. 
As of June 2026, about 235 FMEs were registered in GIFT City, managing over 400 schemes 
with cumulative non-retail fund management commitments of around $45 billion. 
 
PFRDA launches NPS Central, a unified platform where subscribers can manage pension 
accounts across all three recordkeeping agencies 
Key Points 
The Pension Fund Regulatory and Development Authority (PFRDA) has launched NPS Central 
at www.npscentral.in on October 1, 2026, on NPS Diwas. It brings together services of the three 
Central Recordkeeping Agencies (CRAs), namely Protean, KFintech and CAMS,  on one 
interface. 
Existing subscribers can log in with their registered mobile number and date of birth, verified by 
an OTP, so they no longer need to remember their Permanent Retirement Account Number 
(PRAN). 
The mobile number used for login must be the one registered with the account. 
Existing subscribers can check investments across all three CRAs, make and track contributions, 
explore schemes, update profile details, download statements, and raise grievances through the 
Pension Sahayak portal. 
The consolidated dashboard is most useful for subscribers whose accounts are serviced by 
different CRAs. 
New subscribers can register through AI -assisted onboarding, complete KYC through Central 
KYC (CKYC) or DigiLocker, and receive an electronic PRAN (e-PRAN). Onboarding charges are 
nil, subject to applicable terms, but scheme charges still apply. 
The platform also facilitates registration for NPS Swasthya, which combines a dedicated 
investment corpus with a super top -up health insurance arrangement. The article does not give 
its eligibility, cover limits or other conditions. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
47 
 
PFRDA plans to add more services in later phases, including changes to investment choices and 
Pension Funds, Loan Against NPS, Systematic Investment Plan (SIP) mandates, NPS Vatsalya 
accounts, corporate onboarding, Re-KYC and NPS Tier II accounts. No launch dates were given. 
The regulator also plans to let other ecosystem participants offer NPS services through their own 
channels, with API specifications published on the website.

Sebi revises closing auction session: 30-minute VWAP settlement retained, CAS moves to 
3:30-3:40 pm from December 7, 2026 
Key Points 
Sebi has revised the closing auction session (CAS) framework, changing the settlement 
methodology, trading timings and the transition of unexecuted iceberg orders. Most changes take 
effect from December 7, 2026, while the transition of unexecuted limit and  iceberg orders from 
the continuous trading session (CTS) to CAS starts on February 8, 2027. 
The settlement price of index derivatives continues to use the VWAP of constituent securities 
during the last 30 minutes of CTS (3:00 pm to 3:30 pm). 
For expiring stock derivatives, clearing corporations will use the VWAP of the underlying stock 
during the last 30 minutes of CTS across all stock exchanges. 
Exchanges will share the VWAP and traded quantity with clearing corporations and disseminate 
indicative values in real time during this window. 
Under the revised timings, CAS runs as a separate 10 -minute session from 3:30 pm to 3:40 pm 
on all trading days. The order-entry period closes randomly between 3:38 pm and 3:40 pm. 
Index derivatives trade until 3:45 pm on regular days, but contracts expiring that day stop at 3:30 
pm. 
Stock derivatives trade until 3:45 pm, including on expiry days. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
48 
 
The post-close session in the cash segment runs from 3:50 pm to 4 pm, with trades executed at 
closing prices. 
CAS became effective on August 3 and drew complaints from traders over settlement prices, 
sharp price spikes and low liquidity. Sebi received over 20,000 suggestions on its consultation 
paper. It had proposed a blended mechanism, which drew concerns over low liquidity, and traders 
and brokers preferred the 30-minute VWAP method. 
Unexecuted orders will move from CTS to CAS as follows. 
Pending limit orders are generally carried forward, except stop -loss orders and orders priced 
outside the applicable CAS price bands. 
Unexecuted iceberg orders within the price bands convert into fully disclosed limit orders at a 
fixed price, unless participants opt out. These carry lower time priority than limit orders carried 
forward from CTS, but higher priority than limit orders placed during CAS. 
Sebi dropped its proposal to bar cancellation of limit orders placed beyond a 1 per cent range of 
the reference price during CAS. 
 
Sebi asks Irdai to add insurance data to the consolidated account statement, so investors 
can see all their financial assets in one place 
Key Points 
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Sebi) has written to the chairman of the Insurance 
Regulatory and Development Authority of India (Irdai) seeking to include insurance -related 
information in the consolidated account statement (CAS). The aim is to give investors a complete 
view of their financial holdings. 
Sebi chairman Tuhin Kanta Pandey said this at the sidelines of Sahamati's Annual Conference. 
He said the account aggregator framework could help integrate information across financial 
products. 
The consolidated account statement currently covers securities holdings, mutual funds and stocks 
held across brokers, along with the National Pension System (NPS). 
Following an RBI announcement that Sebi -regulated depositories can access bank deposit 
information continuously, Sebi is working on the technical details to include bank deposits as well. 
The expanded statement would support financial planning and investment advice based on an 
investor's overall portfolio. 
On the merchant discount rate (MDR), Sebi is still in talks with the National Payments Corporation 
of India (NPCI) after NPCI deferred the fee. Discussions on whether Sebi made a representation 
on behalf of capital market participants are ongoing. 
Sebi has sent nearly 440 million awareness messages over the last nine days, using digital 
platforms and multilingual communication. 
Sebi is also examining ways to strengthen the securities lending and borrowing (SLB) mechanism, 
which supports short selling. A working group is evaluating proposals, and a consultation paper 
will follow once they are finalised. No timeline has been given.

SEBI Notifies Common Advertisement Code for Celebrity Endorsements 
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) notified a Common Advertisement Code 
(CAC) allowing market intermediaries to use celebrities for brand-level promotion while restricting 
endorsements of specific financial products or services. 
The rules apply to stockbrokers, depository participants, investment advisers, research analysts, 
portfolio managers, online bond platform providers and mutual funds/asset management 
companies. 
Celebrity advertisements will require prior approval from the relevant supervisory body or SEBI, 
where no such body is specified. Endorsement expenses cannot be passed on to clients or mutual 
fund schemes. 
The framework will come into force on April 8, 2027. SEBI also extended the deadline for revising 
IPO issue sizes to March 31, 2027.

Key Points 
Sebi has extended the deadline by six months, to April 27, 2027, for debenture trustees (DTs) to 
comply with requirements on activities outside Sebi's regulatory purview. 
Regulation 9C of the Sebi (Debenture Trustees) Regulations, 1993 requires DTs to transfer such

SEBI Waives Margins on Subsequent Trades Against Early Pay-in 
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) introduced a real -time margin waiver 
framework allowing clients to use the value of shares delivered through Early Pay-in (EPI) towards 
margin requirements on subsequent buy and sell positions in the cash market. 
The revised framework will come into effect from February 22, 2027, four months after the 
circular's issuance. 
Clearing Corporations will calculate the available EPI value on the trade date, considering the 
lower of the value of securities for which EPI was made and the value of securities sold. 
The facility applies where a client has a net sell obligation in a settlement. 
If the client squares off the sale position for which the EPI benefit was used, the EPI value will be 
removed. Any resulting margin shortfall will be blocked from the proprietary collateral of the 
trading or Clearing Member. This condition does not apply to trade-for-trade securities.

SEBI and Bank of England Sign MoU on Cross-Border Clearing Cooperation 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
54 
 
The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI) signed an MoU with the Bank of England 
(BoE) on October 28, 2026, to strengthen regulatory cooperation and information sharing 
concerning SEBI-recognised Clearing Corporations. 
The agreement aims to facilitate safe international clearing operations and protect the financial 
stability of the United Kingdom. 
It establishes a cooperation framework for Central Counterparties (CCPs), allowing the Bank of 
England to rely on SEBI's regulatory and supervisory activities, subject to applicable laws. 
In India, Clearing Corporations are regulated by SEBI under the Securities Contracts (Regulation) 
Act, 1956, and the SEBI (Stock Exchanges and Clearing Corporations) Regulations, 2018. 
Clearing Corporations act as Central Counterparties, ensuring settlement of trades and reducing 
the risk of default between buyers and sellers.
