Urea Import Prices Fall Nearly 10%, Offering Relief to Farmers 
India received urea import offers nearly 10% lower than the prices paid in its August tender, 
providing relief to farmers amid high agricultural input costs. 
Indian Potash Ltd. (IPL) received offers of $356.25 –$416.50 per tonne for the west coast and 
$352.10–$465 per tonne for the east coast. 
Urea prices had surged to as high as $959 per tonne when the Iran conflict began earlier in 2026, 
but subsequently declined as supply constraints eased and global demand weakened. 
Lower prices come ahead of the winter sowing season, when demand for urea is expected to rise 
for crops such as wheat, rapeseed and chickpeas. 
The decline in import prices could provide relief to farmers and reduce the government's fertiliser 
subsidy burden.

Fertiliser sales plunge 37.56% in select border districts after farmer-ID checks 
Key Points 
Fertiliser sales fell 37.56% in select border districts between the kharif seasons of 2025 and 2026, 
after purchases were linked to farmer IDs, crop requirements and land records through AgriStack 
and other digital platforms. 
The decline ranged from 17.35% in Meghalaya's border districts to 81.42% in Tripura's. 
Sales fell 24.84% in other pilot districts, which officials say suggests curbed diversion. 
The linkage is called the Framework for Fertiliser Sales (FFS), which links AgriStack farmer IDs 
with the Integrated Fertiliser Management System (iFMS), run by the Department of Fertilisers. 
FFS is a pilot in about 268 districts across 30 states. 
Urea sells in India at about ₹265 per 45 kg bag, far below the landed global price of about ₹1,674. 
The fertiliser subsidy bill could reach ₹3 trillion in FY27, against a Budget Estimate of about ₹1.7 
trillion. 
AgriStack, developed by the Ministry of Agriculture, has three registries: the Farmer Registry, 
Geo-Referenced Village Maps and the Crop Sown Registry. 
About 102.8 million farmer IDs had been created by July 30, 2026, against a target of 110 million.

Territory in October 2026.

National 
 
Govt Extends RoDTEP and RoSCTL Export Tax Remission Schemes Till December 31, 
2026 
Key Points 
• The government extended its two key export tax remission schemes, RoDTEP and RoSCTL, 
by three months till December 31, 2026.  
• Both schemes were set to end on the day of the announcement, so the extension 
prevents a lapse. 
• The extension was announced on September 30, 2026 , through a government press 
release. 
• Both schemes reimburse exporters for embedded, non -creditable central, state and local 
levies paid on inputs.  
• RoSCTL applies only to textile exports, while RoDTEP covers all other products. 
• The schemes are based on the principle that taxes should not be exported, making 
exports competitive through zero-rating. 
• The government said the extension will provide policy continuity and predictability  for 
exporters.  
• It is intended to sustain the competitiveness of labour-intensive sectors such as apparel 
and made-ups. 
• Experts want a longer extension of five years to give exporters certainty.  


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
60 
 
• Short extensions make it hard for exporters to price orders, since they finalise orders 
months before shipment. 
• The Commerce Ministry had also sought a five -year extension, but the final decision 
rests with the Finance Ministry. 
• Both schemes have been extended several times before, most recently by six months in 
March 2026 to support exporters amid the West Asia conflict. 
 
Cabinet Approves ₹1.86 Trillion Green Energy Corridor Phase -III with 50 GWh Battery 
Storage 
Key Points 
• The Union Cabinet approved the ₹1.86 trillion Green Energy Corridor Phase-III (GEC-III) 
scheme to strengthen the intra-state transmission system and integrate renewable energy 
into the grid.  
• The scheme is targeted for completion by 2032-33, with a total project outlay of 
₹1,86,405 crore. 
• Of the outlay, ₹1,36,378 crore is for Intra-State Transmission Systems (InSTS)  and 
₹50,000 crore is for battery energy storage systems (BESS). 
• The scheme aims to enable evacuation of 135 GW of renewable energy and deploy 50 GWh 
of BESS for grid flexibility.  
• BESS will be deployed at the renewable energy developer or generator end, or at any 
other location of importance. 
• The storage will address intermittency, congestion, peak-hour curtailment, and meet 
demand during non-solar hours. 
• The scheme involves a Central Financial Support of ₹54,082 crore, which will offset intra-
state transmission charges to keep power costs down.  
• The support includes ₹45,005 crore  for intra-state transmission systems and ₹6,000 
crore for BESS through Viability Gap Funding (VGF). 
• ₹3,050 crore  goes towards committed liabilities of earlier phases, and ₹27 crore  for 
programme management and grid studies. 
• The project is expected to leverage about ₹1.32 lakh crore  of investment, along with 
investment linked to 135 GW of renewable capacity estimated at ₹4.6 lakh crore. 
• Procurement and implementation follow defined models.  
• Greenfield InSTS projects will be awarded through Tariff-Based Competitive Bidding 
(TBCB), while brownfield upgradation will be executed on a Cost-Plus Basis (CPB). 
• State Transmission Utilities  will be the implementing agencies, with Transmission 
Service Providers (TSPs) participating under a build-own-operate-maintain (BOOM) 
model. 
• The scheme supports the national target of 900 GW of installed non-fossil capacity by 2035.  
• The government's working targets are 500 GW of non-fossil capacity by 2030 and 786 
GW by 2035-36. 
• The guiding principle is that transmission must precede renewable energy 
generation. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
61 
 
 
VB-G RAM G: Work Demand From Households Rises 34% to 15.64 Million in September 
Key Points 
• Households demanding work under the VB-G RAM G  scheme rose 34% year-on-year to 
15.64 million in September 2026.  
• The scheme, launched on July 1, 2026 , replaced MGNREGA under the name Viksit 
Bharat Guarantee for Rozgar and Ajeevika Mission (Grameen). 
• Work demand was 26.1% higher than the 12.4 million households who sought work in 
August 2026. 
• Experts link the sharp rise in demand to an uneven monsoon that has reduced agricultural 
activity in western and southern states.  
• Maharashtra and Karnataka were cited as states where farm activity dropped. 
• Sources also said demand may reflect the scheme settling down and states ending their 
sowing pauses. 
• Total work generated in September was lower than last year, even as demand rose.  
• About 8.65 crore persondays of work were generated in September 2026. 
• This compares with 11.99 crore persondays in September 2025. 
• Experts say the scheme's true test will come when drought-induced work demand begins 
to rise in the coming months. 
 
Union Cabinet Approves ₹10,000 Crore Commitment to SME Growth Fund for Growth -
Stage Equity 
Key Points 
• The Union Cabinet approved a ₹10,000 crore government commitment to an SME Growth 
Fund, which will provide direct equity investments  to small and medium enterprises 
(SMEs), with a focus on manufacturing.  
• The commitment was announced as part of the 2026-27 Union Budget. 
• The fund will be set up as an alternative investment fund (AIF). 
• The fund aims to fill a gap in growth-stage equity financing, as existing equity funds mostly 
focus on early-stage businesses and micro enterprises.  
• It will provide long-term capital to businesses with established operations and potential 
to scale. 
• Funding can be used to expand capacity, adopt technology, enter international markets, 
undertake acquisitions and integrate into global value chains.  
• The government expects investments to help SMEs improve productivity, increase 
exports and participate in global supply chains. 
• A majority of the allocation will go to SMEs, including those in industrial clusters in Tier-II 
and Tier-III cities.  
• Investments in smaller -city clusters are expected to support local supply chains and 
employment. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
62 
 
• The government has also highlighted other MSME measures, including credit support , 
digitalisation, public procurement reforms , startup initiatives  and production-linked 
incentive (PLI) schemes. 
 
VB-G RAM G Permissible Works Raised 41% Over MGNREGA, With New Disaster -Relief 
Categories 
Key Points 
• The Centre has raised the number of permissible works under VB-G RAM G  to 375, up 
41% from the 266 allowed under MGNREGA.  
• The scheme replaced MGNREGA across India from July 1, 2026. 
• For the first time, 38 dedicated special categories  have been added for extreme 
weather events and disaster preparedness. 
• The disaster -related works include flood relief shelters , retaining walls , embankment 
strengthening, flood protection, post-disaster restoration of rural roads and community 
assets, and forest-fire management measures such as fire lines and fuel-buffer zones. 
• The schedule is organised under four pillars.  
• Water security has the largest share, with 128 works, including check dams, recharge 
shafts, rejuvenation of rivers and water bodies , drip irrigation  and rooftop 
rainwater harvesting. 
• Core rural infrastructure has 111 works, covering items such as solar street lighting, 
common service centres and Jal Jeevan Mission repairs. 
• Livelihood-related infrastructure has 98 works, including cold storage , mini flour 
mills, dairy infrastructure and aquaculture structures. 
• Special works for extreme weather and disaster preparedness  make up the fourth 
pillar. 
• Livelihood works now account for 26% of permissible works, compared with 1.1% under the 
previous framework. 
• Of the 375 works, 181 are agriculture and allied works, 107 target Antyodaya and vulnerable 
households, and 36 each relate to women and rural youth. 
• All works will originate through bottom-up planning under Viksit Gram Panchayat Plans  
and require Gram Sabha approval.  
• Implementation will be tracked through the Viksit Bharat National Rural Infrastructure 
Stack. 
• Around 15.64 million households  sought work under the scheme in September 2026 , 
nearly 34% more than the same month last year under MGNREGA.

Government reduces windfall tax on diesel and ATF exports 
• The Ministry of Finance revised export levies on diesel and Aviation Turbine Fuel (ATF) on 
September 30, 2026, with the new rates effective from October 1, 2026. 
• Diesel export duty was reduced from ₹20 to ₹16 per litre, while ATF export duty was cut from 
₹15 to ₹10.50 per litre. 
• The export duty on petrol remained unchanged at ₹0.50 per litre for the next fortnight. 
• The government had reintroduced export levies on diesel and ATF in March 2026 and 
extended the levy to petrol exports in May 2026. 
• The measure aims to ensure adequate domestic fuel availability, discourage excessive 
exports and prevent undue gains from fluctuations in global crude oil prices. 
 
MoP Notifies New CAFE-III Norms for Passenger Vehicles 
• The Ministry of Power notified Corporate Average Fuel Economy (CAFE -III) norms, 
applicable from April 1, 2027, to March 31, 2032, for five years. 
• The fuel-consumption benchmark will decline from 3.996 litres per 100 km in 2027 –28 to 
3.3273 litres per 100 km in 2031–32, representing an improvement of nearly 16.7%. 
• The reference vehicle weight has increased from 1,082 kg to 1,229 kg. 
• Super-credit multipliers are 3× for Battery Electric Vehicles (BEVs)/Range-Extended Electric 
Vehicles (REEVs), 2.5× for Plug -in Hybrid Electric Vehicles (PHEVs), and 1.6× for Strong 
Hybrid Electric Vehicles (SHEVs). 
• Carbon Neutrality Factors reduce declared CO₂ emissions by 8% for E20 and higher ethanol-
blended petrol vehicles, 22.3% for flex-fuel ethanol vehicles, and 5% for CNG/CBG vehicles, 
subject to the specified rules. Recognised fuel -efficiency technologies have expanded from 
4 to 12.

AAI Launches ₹165 Crore Procurement to Automate Airport Security Tray Movement 
The Airports Authority of India (AAI) has initiated a procurement process worth ₹164.8 crore 
(excluding GST) to install 52 Automatic Tray Retrieval System (ATRS) lanes at various airports. 
The bidding deadline is October 22, 2026. 
ATRS automates the movement of trays through airport security checkpoints using conveyors 
and mechanical equipment, helping reduce passenger waiting time and allowing security 
personnel to focus on baggage screening. 
The procurement includes ₹98.9 crore for supply and installation and ₹65.9 crore for seven years 
of maintenance. It also provides for a one-year onsite warranty, staff training and spare parts. 
The systems will include analyst workstations, tray collection stations and separate lanes for 
baggage requiring additional examination. Cybersecurity testing, including Vulnerability 
Assessment and Penetration Testing (VAPT), is also mandatory. 
The initiative follows a direction by Union Home Minister Amit Shah on July 8 to introduce ATRS 
in a phased manner across airports nationwide. He also called for prioritising 62 airports —21 
under development and 41 other hubs—with work to be completed within two years. 
 
Supreme Court Mandates Insurance for Railway Counter Tickets from October 27, 2026 
The Supreme Court of India has directed Indian Railways to provide travel insurance to 
passengers purchasing tickets at railway station counters from October 27, 2026, on the same 
terms as online bookings. 
The direction was issued by a bench comprising Justices Ahsanuddin Amanullah and R. 
Mahadevan on October 6, 2026, while hearing a plea related to the June 2023 Odisha train 
accident. 
The Court made the order mandatory and held the Railway Board directly accountable for any 
failure to implement it, ensuring parity between online and offline ticket purchasers. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
71 
 
The proceedings also examined passenger safety concerns, including the implementation of the 
Kavach train protection system, track fencing, construction of foot overbridges and 
underutilisation of safety-related budget allocations. 
The matter is scheduled for the next hearing on November 16, 2026. The move aims to extend 
financial protection to passengers, particularly those from economically disadvantaged sections 
who commonly purchase tickets at railway counters. 
 
MoSPI Releases Strategy for Environmental Economic Accounts in India 2026–2030 
In October 2026, MoSPI released the Strategy for Environmental Economic Accounts in India 
2026–2030 to strengthen environmental accounting and integrate environmental and economic 
data. 
The strategy was released by the Social Statistics Division under the National Statistics Office 
(NSO). 
India’s environmental accounting framework is aligned with the United Nations System of 
Environmental-Economic Accounting (SEEA). 
SEEA supports the assessment of around 40 global indicators linked to 9 Sustainable 
Development Goals (SDGs). 
The new strategy succeeds the 2022 –2026 strategy and identifies around 10 priority areas, 
including forests, water, land, soil, minerals, carbon stock, biodiversity, pollination services, 
environment-related economic activities and expanded environmental statistics. 
 
NSO Releases Findings of India’s First-Ever National Household Travel Survey 
In October 2026, the National Statistics Office (NSO), under the Ministry of Statistics and 
Programme Implementation (MoSPI), released findings of the National Household Travel Survey 
(NHTS), India’s first nationwide survey on household travel patterns. 
Student Mobility: 66.4% of students aged 6 years and above travel more than 1 km daily to 
educational institutions. The figure is 65.6% in rural areas and 68.1% in urban areas. 
Mode of Travel: Walking is the most common mode of travel for students (37%), followed by 
buses (29.5%) and bicycles (15.8%). 
Workplace Mobility: 42.6% of workers use two -wheelers to commute to fixed workplaces. The 
share is 37.1% in rural areas and 52.3% in urban areas. 
Commuting Expenditure: Average monthly commuting expenditure is ₹785 per worker, 
comprising ₹1,044 in urban areas and ₹612 in rural areas.

Philip Morris India and Litterati Launch Anti-Littering Project Unmesha 
In October 2026, Philip Morris India Trading Private Limited partnered with global civic technology 
company Litterati to launch the first phase of a 12 -month anti-littering initiative in Delhi under 
Project Unmesha. 
The Delhi pilot was launched on 5 October 2026 and focuses on environmental stewardship, 
circularity and climate resilience. 
Technology: Litterati will use community science and AI-enabled image recognition to identify litter 
hotspots, map waste composition and establish scientific baselines. 
Clean-up Results: The #CleanWithBittu initiative removed over 4,000 kg of litter. More than 1,600 
kg was diverted for recycling or composting, avoiding an estimated 2,200 kg of CO ₂-equivalent 
emissions. 
The programme aims to develop a replicable, data -driven model combining litter mapping,

Swachh Bharat Mission. 
 
MHA Notifies Central Armed Police Forces (General Administration) Act, 2026 
The Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) notified the Central Armed Police Forces (General 
Administration) Act, 2026, which came into force on 8 October 2026. 
The Act governs recruitment, promotion, deputation and other service conditions in five Central 
Armed Police Forces (CAPFs): CRPF, BSF, CISF, ITBP and SSB. 
Deputation Provisions: 50% of Inspector General (IG) posts are reserved for IPS deputation, while 
at least 67% of Additional Director General (ADG) posts are to be filled through IPS deputation. 
Posts at the Special Director General and Director General ranks are to be filled exclusively 
through deputation. 
Before the new Act, recruitment rules provided for 20% of Deputy Inspector General (DIG) posts 
and 50% of IG posts to be filled through deputation. 
The Act's validity has been challenged in the Supreme Court.

2026 under the chairpersonship of Union Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman.

In-kind welfare transfers lifted about 220 million Indians into a higher consumption class 
in 2022-23, with West Bengal the top beneficiary, says NSO study 
Key Points 
A paper in Sarvekshana, the journal of the National Statistical Office (NSO), finds that free and 
subsidised goods from government welfare schemes improved the living standards of about 220 
million Indians in 2022-23. 
Once the value of these in-kind transfers is counted, 15.8 per cent of the total population moved 
into a higher consumption class. 
The effect was much stronger in villages, where 18.8 per cent of the rural population moved up, 
than in cities, where 8.4 per cent did. 
The benefits come from food grains through the Public Distribution System (PDS), pulses, sugar, 
salt and edible oil, along with school uniforms, school shoes, bicycles, mobile phones, tablets and 
laptops. 
West Bengal recorded the highest share of beneficiaries among the 18 major states, with 40.5 
per cent of its population moving into a higher class. 
It led in both areas, with 46.5 per cent of its rural population and 24.9 per cent of its urban 
population moving up. 
At the other end, Gujarat (3.7 per cent) and Kerala (4.6 per cent) recorded the smallest shares. 
The study used unit -level data from the NSO's Household Consumption Expenditure Survey 
(HCES) 2022 -23. Households were ranked by monthly per capita consumption expenditure 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
79 
 
(MPCE) without the transfers and sorted into 12 classes, from the poorest 5 per cent to the richest 
5 per cent. 
The value of the free items was then added at common unit prices, and any household that moved 
up a class was counted as having an improved standard of living. 
Most of the upward movement happened at the bottom of the distribution, which the authors read 
as evidence that the transfers are progressive and reach the poor rather than the rich. 
In rural India, the share moving up one class ranged from 4 to 33 per cent, with the peak in the 5-
10 per cent class. 
In urban India, it ranged from 1 to 22 per cent, again highest in the second-lowest class. 
The poorest classes gained the most in 12 states in rural areas and 15 states in urban areas. 
Some schemes hand out bicycles, laptops, phones and uniforms regardless of income, which 
probably explains some movement among better-off households. 
The transfers also made consumption slightly more equal. The Gini coefficient, where a lower 
figure means more equal distribution, fell from 0.266 to 0.261 in rural India and from 0.314 to 
0.309 in urban India. 
The authors describe the change as modest but say it shows public transfers have an equalising 
effect, and they expect the gains to reduce poverty when official poverty estimates are next made. 
 
NMDC and SAIL set up subsidiaries at GIFT City to secure overseas critical minerals and 
manage group finances 
Key Points 
State-owned NMDC Ltd and Steel Authority of India Ltd (SAIL) have incorporated subsidiaries at 
the International Financial Services Centre (IFSC) in GIFT City, according to Ministry of Corporate 
Affairs (MCA) records. The move supports India's push to secure critical mineral supplies abroad. 
NMDC Global IFSC Limited is proposed as an investment and financial platform for overseas 
mineral projects, particularly critical and strategic minerals. 
SAIL Global IFSC Limited is set up mainly as a group treasury centre to manage cash, intra-group 
lending, currency and trade finance. 
The NMDC subsidiary will support investments, acquisitions, joint ventures and equity stakes in 
overseas projects involving lithium, cobalt, nickel, copper, gold, coking coal and iron ore. Lithium, 
cobalt, nickel and copper are among the minerals the Indian government has classified as critical. 
NMDC has been evaluating acquisitions across 10 strategic mineral assets globally. 
SAIL imported around 85 per cent of the coal it consumed in the June quarter. 
Once fully operational, the subsidiaries could let the companies borrow in dollars, hedge currency 
risk by locking in exchange rates for future payments, and manage group cash centrally. 
Experts say they could also help PSUs that import coal or iron ore manage freight costs through 
long-term vessel arrangements. 
Earlier, such companies would typically have used international financial hubs such as Singapore 
or Dubai. 
Experts caution that GIFT City will not automatically lower borrowing or trade -finance costs. The 
benefits depend on deal volumes, credit strength and the net cost after tax. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
80 
 
The subsidiaries may later move into commodity trading, which needs regulatory approval. SAIL's 
memorandum allows commodity derivatives and metal hedging, but not buying and selling 
physical commodities. 
The International Financial Services Centres Authority (IFSCA) has said talks are underway to 
classify commodity trading as a financial product at the hub.

The Ministry of Mines has appointed nodal officers in Indian missions across 13 countries to

abroad. 
 
Three New Sites Added to India’s UNESCO Tentative List of World Heritage Sites 
UNESCO has added three sites to India’s Tentative List of World Heritage Sites: Sirpur Cultural 
and Archaeological Landscape (Chhattisgarh), Baratang Karst -Mud Volcano Complex, and 
Andaman Subduction Volcanoes (Narcondam and Barren Islands). 
With these additions, India’s UNESCO Tentative List has increased to 81 sites. 
Sirpur, located in Mahasamund district, Chhattisgarh, lies on the eastern bank of the Mahanadi 
River, around 78 km from Raipur. It reflects the region’s rich archaeological, architectural and 
multi-faith heritage. 
The Baratang site features limestone caves and active mud volcanoes, while Narcondam and 
Barren Islands are known for their volcanic formations, ecological succession and endemic 
biodiversity. 
Inclusion in the Tentative List is a preliminary step towards consideration for UNESCO World 
Heritage status.

plant, Kalinganagar (KNR 1), in Odisha, adding an annual production capacity of 1 lakh tonnes.

it India’s largest producer of value-added ferro chrome. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
81 
 
The company aims to achieve an annual ferro chrome output of 5 lakh tonnes by FY28. 
The plant uses closed -furnace technology and an 8.7 MW power generation unit that recovers 
energy from furnace gases to improve efficiency and reduce emissions. 
Ferro chrome, an alloy of chromium and iron, is a key raw material used in stainless steel 
production. The project is expected to support around 1,500 direct and indirect jobs.

framework.

compliance through data analytics, better return filing and invoice matching.

Ministry of Cooperation Mandates MSCARO 2026 for Multi-State Cooperative Societies 
The Ministry of Cooperation introduced the Multi-State Co-operative Societies (Auditor’s Report) 
Order, 2026 (MSCARO 2026) to strengthen transparency, accountability and financial discipline. 
The framework is based on the Companies (Auditor’s Report) Order and is issued under Section 
73(6) of the Multi-State Co-operative Societies (Amendment) Act, 2023. 
Auditors must report on financial, administrative, governance and statutory compliance 
parameters in a standardised format, including Yes/No responses with remarks. 
Initially, it will cover larger societies exceeding ₹1,000 crore in relevant financial parameters, with 
plans to extend it to societies crossing the prescribed ₹50 crore threshold. 
 
MoRTH Proposes Phase-wise Rollout of BS-VII Emission Standards from 2029 
The Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH) proposed the phased introduction of 
Bharat Stage VII (BS -VII) emission standards, aligned with European Union Euro 7 norms, for 
new cars and SUVs from April 1, 2029. 
The rollout will take place in three phases: April 2029, April 2031 and April 2035, introducing 
progressively stricter limits on brake particles, tyre abrasion and particulate emissions. 
The permitted particulate matter size threshold is proposed to be reduced from 23 nanometres to 
10 nanometres. 
The timeline for implementing BS-VII standards for two-wheelers will be announced separately.

UDAN Scheme Completes 10 Years of Implementation 
The Regional Connectivity Scheme UDAN (Ude Desh ka Aam Nagrik), launched on 21 October 
2016 under the Ministry of Civil Aviation (MoCA), completed 10 years of implementation. 
The scheme aims to make air travel affordable and improve connectivity to unserved and 
underserved regions, particularly Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities. 
The Airports Authority of India (AAI) is the nodal implementing agency. 
UDAN has operationalised 687 regional routes and 95 aerodromes, including 17 heliports and 
two water aerodromes, facilitating over 3.68 lakh flights and benefiting 1.72 crore passengers. 
The modified UDAN scheme, launched in July 2026, has an outlay of ₹28,840 crore for FY2026–
27 to FY2035–36. 
 
MHA Restructures CISF into Sector-Specific Functional Commands 
The Ministry of Home Affairs approved a major restructuring of the Central Industrial Security 
Force (CISF), effective 1 November 2026. 
The restructuring will shift the force from territorial supervision towards specialised, sector-specific 
functional commands to improve security at critical infrastructure and strategic establishments. 
Eight of the CISF’s 11 territorial sectors will be reorganised, including dedicated sectors for power, 
coal, steel, petroleum and natural gas, and ports. 
The Department of Atomic Energy and Department of Space sector and both airport sectors will 
retain their existing arrangements.

NSO to add a separate sampling stratum for digital platform workers and new unpaid-work 
questions in PLFS from January 2027 
Key Points 
The NSO has proposed a separate sampling stratum for households with digital platform workers 
(such as Ola, Uber, Zomato, Amazon and Urban Company) in the Periodic Labour Force Survey 
(PLFS) from January 2027. 
A binary question on the household listing schedule will ask whether any member did digital

Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
87 
 
New questions will measure unpaid household work, caregiving, volunteering, unpaid trainee

an experimental basis. 
The changes will apply from Panel 6 (the 2027 -28 cycle), when the current Panel 5 ends in 
December 2026. 
From January to March 2027, estimates will use pooled data from Panels 5 and 6; from April 
2027, monthly estimates will use only Panel 6. 
The proposals are part of a concept note released by the Ministry of Statistics and Programme 
Implementation (Mospi). The PLFS has been conducted by the NSO since 2017 and was last 
revamped in January 2025. 
 
Cabinet approves ₹10,000 crore Container Manufacturing Promotion Scheme (CMPS) 
Key Points 
The Cabinet approved the ₹10,000 crore Container Manufacturing Promotion Scheme (CMPS), 
announced in the Budget in February, to run till 2039-40 (FY40). 
It aims to raise annual domestic container manufacturing capacity about ten -fold, to around 
750,000 TEUs. 
It is expected to create about 3,000 direct jobs and over 50,000 indirect jobs. 
The scheme has three components: 
Interest subvention assistance of ₹859 crore, giving 3% per annum on eligible term loans for 
greenfield and brownfield container manufacturing facilities. 
Operational expenditure assistance of ₹8,891 crore, for containers made to global shipping 
standards and sold by eligible manufacturers. 
Testing and certification assistance of ₹150 crore, for common facilities such as dynamic 
longitudinal impact testing. 
The scheme aims to bridge the cost gap with China, since global shipping lines buy most 
containers from China. 
 
Cabinet approves ₹7,395 crore scheme to make gram panchayats self-reliant 
Key Points 
The Cabinet has approved a ₹7,395 crore scheme to transform over 2.5 lakh gram panchayats 
into financially empowered, self-reliant, accountable and high-performing institutions of local self-
government. 
The scheme shifts from prescription -based schemes to local capacity -building and planning, 
based on each panchayat's own resources and needs. 
The scheme focuses on general and specialised training for elected representatives, better use 
of Finance Commission grants, and mobilisation of own-source revenue (OSR). 
OSR contributes between 1% and 40% of panchayats' total annual receipts. 
Limited economic activity, low incomes and land scarcity limit OSR generation.

Government extends customs duty exemption on cotton imports till December 31, 2026

Simplified GST registration for small e-commerce sellers to start from November 1 
Key Points 
The simplified GST registration facility, recommended by the 57th GST Council, will go live on the 
GST portal from November 1, 2026. 
It is an optional route under the new Rule 14B of the CGST Rules, 2017. 
Small sellers of goods who supply only through e -commerce operators (ECOs) can register in 
other states by declaring the platform's warehouse as their principal place of business, without 
setting up their own office. 
Eligibility conditions: 
The seller must pass on input tax credit (ITC) of not more than ₹2.5 lakh a month. 
The seller must already have a physical presence and GST registration in at least one state, which 
remains the home state. 
The registration can be used only for supplies made through e-commerce platforms. 
A committee of officers will study Section 16(2)(c) of the CGST Act, which links a buyer's ITC to 
the supplier's actual payment of tax, and report within three months. Any decision is targeted for 
implementation from April 1, 2027. 
 
GSTN adds two e-invoicing categories for reverse charge purchases 
Key Points 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
89 
 
The GSTN has introduced two new e -invoicing categories, following a recommendation of the 
57th GST Council meeting, to report self-invoices for specified reverse charge mechanism (RCM) 
transactions. 
RCM-DOM is for specified domestic supplies from unregistered persons. 
RCM-IMP is for imports of services. 
Eligible businesses can generate Invoice Reference Numbers (IRNs) for self -invoices through 
authorised Invoice Registration Portals (IRPs). 
The facility is for registered taxpayers with an aggregate annual turnover of ₹50 million (₹5 crore) 
and above. 
It is the first time e-invoices will be generated on specified purchases instead of sales. 
Self-invoices under RCM must be issued within 30 days. 
The facility is available for testing on all six authorised e-invoice portals, and the production rollout 
date is yet to be announced.

CBDT Introduces New Form 173A for Reporting Tax Exemption Claims 
The Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT), under the Ministry of Finance, introduced Form 173A 
for reporting specified income-tax exemption claims on interest and capital gains from government 
securities. 
The form was notified under the Income -tax (Seventh Amendment) Rules, 2026, and became 
effective on October 19, 2026. 
CBDT inserted Rule 281A into the Income-tax Rules, 2026, for reporting exemption claims under 
Schedule IV, Table Serial Numbers 13D and 13E of the Income-tax Act, 2025. 
The form is applicable to specified Foreign Institutional Investors (FIIs) and the Bank for 
International Settlements (BIS), subject to prescribed conditions. 
Form 173A has two parts: Part A covers investor details, including PAN and registration 
information, while Part B covers securities, holdings, transactions, interest income and capital 
gains.

MoRTH Proposes Mandatory Advanced Emergency Braking Systems for New Cars from 
2030 
The Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH) issued a draft notification proposing 
mandatory Advanced Emergency Braking Systems (AEBS) for all new M1 -category car models 
from October 1, 2030. 
The proposed systems must comply with Automotive Industry Standard (AIS) -185. Existing car 
models already in production before the deadline will have to comply from October 1, 2032. 
For trucks, AEBS requirements will come into force from October 1, 2027, along with Electronic 
Stability Control (ESC) systems conforming to AIS-162. 
The ministry also directed motor vehicles to comply with AIS -193, with motorcycles and three -
wheelers without steering wheels exempted. 
Automatic Command Steering Functionalities (ACSF) and Emergency Lane Keeping Systems 
(ELKS) will also be subject to specified safety standards, becoming mandatory for M1 transport 
vehicles from October 1, 2029.

MoRTH Allows E100 Ethanol Fuel in Vehicles Designed for Flexible Fuel 
The Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH) amended the Central Motor Vehicles 
Rules, 1989, allowing the use of ethanol -based fuels containing up to 100% ethanol (E100) in 
vehicles designed for flexible fuel. 
Both E85 and E100 fuels can be used only in vehicles specifically designed and certified by 
Original Equipment Manufacturers (OEMs) as Flex-Fuel Vehicles (FFVs). 
The amended rules also expand the use of B100 diesel and Hydrogen -enriched Compressed 
Natural Gas (HCNG) combinations for heavy applications. 
Existing vehicle owners are not required to switch to E85/E100 or modify their vehicles. Vehicles 
compatible with E10 or E20 are not automatically compatible with E85 or E100. 
The amendment also increases the Gross Vehicle Weight (GVW) threshold for light petrol and 
diesel vehicles from 3,000 kg to 3,500 kg. 
 
MoRTH Introduces Annual Concessional Toll Pass for Residents Living Near Fee Plazas 
The Ministry of Road Transport and Highways introduced a Concessional Local Pass for eligible 
non-commercial vehicle owners residing within 20 km of National Highway fee plazas. 
The annual pass will cost ₹1,500 for fee plazas outside municipal or local town limits and ₹3,000 
for plazas within these limits. 
The provision was introduced through the National Highways Fee (Determination of Rates and 
Collection) (Fifth Amendment) Rules, 2026, under the National Highways Act, 1956. 
The existing monthly pass costs ₹360, or ₹4,320 annually. The new pass is valid only for the 
specific fee plaza and cannot be transferred. 
It will be available at eligible toll plazas from November 1, 2026, and through the Rajmargyatra 
application from December 1, 2026. It will not apply to specified closed user -fee collection 
systems or plazas with alternative roads.

The Union Cabinet approved the Khelo India Sports Goods Manufacturing Scheme with an outlay 
of ₹1,856.67 crore for FY 2026–27 to FY 2033–34. 
Implemented by the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports, the scheme aims to strengthen domestic 
sports goods manufacturing, promote innovation and exports, and reduce import dependence.

manufacturing ecosystem. 
The scheme proposes four integrated Sports Goods Manufacturing Zones (SGMZs), each

industrial parks. 
Anchor investors committing at least ₹100 crore can receive 20% capital expenditure support, 
while eligible manufacturers investing ₹10 crore or more can receive turnover-linked incentives of 
up to 15%.

Union Minister C. R. Paatil Launches 'Punah Bharan' App for Groundwater Recharge 
Planning 
Union Minister C. R. Paatil, Ministry of Jal Shakti, launched the Punah Bharan mobile application 
on October 28, 2026, to support scientific groundwater recharge planning. 
The application was developed by the Bhaskaracharya National Institute for Space Applications 
and Geo-informatics (BISAG-N) in collaboration with the National Water Mission (NWM). 
It identifies potential groundwater recharge zones within a 3-km radius and provides village-level 
maps of Artificial Recharge Structures (ARS). 
The platform uses rainfall, groundwater levels, yield, slope and drainage data to identify suitable 
locations for recharge interventions. 
States with the highest share of over -exploited groundwater units include Punjab (75.16%), 
Haryana (61.54%), Delhi (41.18%), Tamil Nadu (33.87%) and Karnataka (18.99%).

The Ayushman Vay Vandana (AVV) healthcare initiative completed two years of implementation 
in October 2026.

ESIC Extends New Amnesty Scheme 2025 Until December 31, 2026 
The Employees' State Insurance Corporation (ESIC) extended the New Amnesty Scheme 2025 
by three months, from September 30 to December 31, 2026. 
The scheme is a one-time dispute resolution initiative aimed at reducing pending court cases and 
encouraging compliance with the Employees' State Insurance Act, 1948. 
Employers can settle ad-hoc assessments by paying actual contributions due along with interest, 
without any damages. 
Pending cases can be withdrawn where employers have already paid their dues, subject to 
payment of 10% of disputed damages. 
Court cases and legal disputes filed up to March 31, 2025, are eligible, subject to the scheme's 
conditions.

India Reaffirms Commitment to Whale Conservation at IWC-70 
• India reaffirmed its commitment to whale and cetacean conservation at the 70th meeting of 
the International Whaling Commission (IWC -70), held in Hobart, Australia, from September 
28 to October 2, 2026. 
• The meeting marked the 80th anniversary of the International Convention for the Regulation 
of Whaling and adopted the Hobart Declaration by consensus.

Dolphin. India joined the IWC in 1981; the commission has 89 member governments.

Mechanism (JCM) in New Delhi in September 2026. 
• The mechanism promotes low-carbon technologies, climate finance, technology cooperation 
and credible greenhouse-gas emission reductions and removals in India.

adoption of the Rules of Implementation on June 8, 2026.

Idea Note to JCM credit issuance and authorisation.

with commissioning targeted within six years of groundbreaking.

West Asia war, debt woes top agenda of IMF-World Bank meetings in Bangkok 
Key Points 
The IMF-World Bank annual meetings  are being held in Bangkok this week, outside 
Washington for the first time in three years.  
The agenda is dominated by the US-Israeli-led war with Iran, now in its eighth month, and the 
inflation and hardship it has caused. 
US Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent skipped the meetings and a G20 meeting, sending two 
senior officials instead. 
Iran's closure of the Strait of Hormuz has shut off around 20% of the world's oil.  
Group of Seven (G7) countries agreed to release 100 million barrels of diesel and crude from 
emergency reserves. 
More than 1 billion barrels of oil have been released since the war began on February 28, mainly 
from onshore commercial inventories. 
Trump announced a deal with Russia that would provide more diesel to global markets, along 
with a temporary waiver of US sanctions. 
The IMF has signalled little change in its forecast of 3% global growth for 2026, and may edge 
its forecast for next year slightly higher.  
Downgrades are expected for Ukraine and Gulf countries  hit by Iranian strikes and sharply 
reduced energy exports. 
The World Bank is not revising down its global forecasts at present. 
The IMF says public debt is at its highest level since World War II and will exceed 100% of GDP 
before 2030.  
Advanced economies, led by the United States, have the highest debt-to-GDP ratios. 
Emerging markets and low -income countries are particularly vulnerable, facing capital outflows, 
El Niño and a lack of investment in AI. 
Interest payments already exceed 10% of revenue in developing countries on average. 
The IMF has recommended fewer but deeper reforms  as a condition for approving loan 
programmes, a change that many lower-income countries worry will lead to austerity. 
In IMF research released on Tuesday, sharp spikes in food and energy prices were identified as 
an increasingly common source of crises. 
Flight routes to Bangkok often pass through the Middle East, and more than 10,000 travellers 
are expected in the city, which follows recent attacks on Saudi airports. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
97 
 
 
India and Singapore Plan 3,000-km Cross-Border Power Link 
India and Singapore are exploring a 2,000 MW cross -border electricity interconnection project 
under the broader ASEAN Power Grid initiative. 
One proposed route is a land-based transmission corridor of approximately 3,000 km, connecting 
Imphal in India to Singapore through Myanmar, Thailand and Malaysia. 
The project aims to promote cross-border electricity trade, integrate clean energy and strengthen 
regional energy security. 
Shripad Yesso Naik, Minister of State for Power and New and Renewable Energy, highlighted the 
initiative at the 20th East Asia Summit Energy Ministers’ Meeting in Manila, Philippines. 
India also emphasised cooperation under the ASEAN–India Plan of Action 2026–2030, including 
renewable energy, regional power transmission, energy storage and clean energy technologies. 
 
WMO Warns El Niño to Intensify, with Peak Expected in December 2026 
The World Meteorological Organization (WMO) has stated that prevailing El Niño conditions in 
the equatorial Pacific Ocean are strengthening rapidly and are expected to peak in December 
2026. 
WMO forecasts a near -100% probability of El Niño persisting through February 2027. It could 
become one of the strongest episodes since at least 1950. 
El Niño is a phase of the El Niño–Southern Oscillation (ENSO), a climate phenomenon involving 
changes in sea surface temperatures in the central and eastern tropical Pacific Ocean and 
associated atmospheric variations. 
India received 87.4% of the Long Period Average (LPA) rainfall during the 2026 southwest 
monsoon season, recording 759.4 mm against the normal of 868.6 mm. 
El Niño can weaken India's monsoon rainfall and increase global temperatures, raising the risk of 
droughts, extreme heat, wildfires and ecological damage. Its opposite phase, La Niña, generally 
has a cooling effect. 
 
American Express Fined $350 Million for Anti-Money Laundering Failures 
US banking regulators, the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) and the Federal 
Reserve, imposed a $350 million fine on American Express for deficiencies in its anti -money 
laundering (AML) compliance system. 
According to the OCC, systemic monitoring and reporting failures led to the inadequate 
identification, evaluation and reporting of approximately $13 billion in suspicious activity over a 
decade. 
The regulators identified shortcomings in staffing, employee training, internal controls, customer 
due diligence and customer identification programmes. 
The OCC also noted that American Express focused more on risks associated with deposit 
products than on its much larger credit card business. 
American Express CEO Stephen Squeri said the company was committed to addressing the 
concerns. The company neither admitted nor denied the regulators’ findings.

and market access across 118 services sectors and sub-sectors.

aim to double bilateral trade by 2030.

Government Approves India's Accession to SIOFA 
The Government of India approved accession to the Southern Indian Ocean Fisheries Agreement 
(SIOFA) to expand access to authorised high-seas fisheries and promote sustainable harvesting 
of marine resources. 
India, which has been a Cooperating Non-Contracting Party since 2022, will gain full participation 
in decision-making through its membership as a Contracting Party. 
Indian fishing vessels will be able to undertake authorised fishing of squid, toothfish, alfonsino 
and other non-migratory fish stocks within SIOFA’s jurisdiction. 
The move aims to strengthen international fisheries cooperation, improve access to scientific 
research and stock assessments, and create opportunities for fishers and marine businesses.

India Introduces Principal Purpose Test in India–Mauritius Tax Treaty 
The Central Government notified amendments to the India–Mauritius Double Taxation Avoidance 
Agreement (DTAA) on October 13, 2026, introducing the Principal Purpose Test (PPT) to curb 
treaty abuse and tax avoidance. 
The amended treaty came into force on August 10, 2026, following a protocol signed by India and 
Mauritius on March 7, 2024. 
Under the PPT, treaty benefits may be denied if obtaining those benefits was one of the principal 
purposes of a transaction or arrangement, unless granting them aligns with the treaty’s objectives. 
Investments made before April 1, 2017, are exempt from the PPT, while Mauritius-based investors 
and funds may face greater scrutiny over the commercial purpose of their transactions.

Japan Launches MMX Mission to Collect Samples from Mars’ Moon Phobos 
The Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) launched the uncrewed Martian Moons 
eXploration (MMX) mission aboard an H3 rocket from Tanegashima Space Center, Japan, in 
October 2026. 
The mission aims to collect at least 10 grams of material from Phobos, one of Mars’ moons, and 
return the samples to Earth in 2031. 
If successful, MMX will become the first mission to return samples from a Martian moon to Earth. 
The spacecraft is expected to reach Mars in August 2027. 
The mission involves NASA, the European Space Agency, France’s CNES and Germany’s DLR. 
It carries the IDEFIX rover, developed by CNES and DLR, for deployment on Phobos.

December 2023; the latter was reportedly deployed operationally in April 2026.

NASA's Perseverance Rover Sets Driving Record on Mars 
NASA's Perseverance rover set a new extraterrestrial driving record by covering 45.22 km (28.06 
miles) on Mars, surpassing the previous record held by NASA's Opportunity rover at 45.16 km. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
107 
 
The achievement was made possible through its Autonomous Navigation (AutoNav) system, 
which uses onboard cameras and computing to detect obstacles and independently plan routes. 
The rover's mission includes studying Martian geology, searching for signs of ancient microbial 
life and collecting rock samples for possible return to Earth. 
Separately, NASA selected Indian astronomer Dr. Ramya Manjunath Anche, from the University 
of Arizona, as Principal Investigator of the Polarimetry in the Ultraviolet to Find Features in 
Interstellar Dust (PUFFINS) mission.

launch in 2030.

signing the Terms of Reference (ToR) in October 2026.

Industry, to Mexico City. 
The agreement will establish the framework, scope, negotiating procedures and roadmap for the 
proposed PTA. 
Negotiations will cover market access for goods, rules of origin, customs procedures, trade 
facilitation, dispute settlement and technical barriers to trade. 
Priority sectors include automobiles, chemicals, electronics, pharmaceuticals, agri -food, energy, 
critical minerals, medical devices and advanced manufacturing.

• Madhya Pradesh Chief Minister Mohan Yadav released two cheetahs, a male named Vyom

Jharkhand's traditional musical instrument 'Mandar' receives GI tag 
• The traditional folk musical instrument Mandar of Jharkhand received a Geographical 
Indication (GI) tag from the Geographical Indications Registry, Chennai, in September 2026. 
• The GI application, numbered 777, was filed on 24 September 2021 by Raidih Mandar 
Producer Company Limited, Gumla, Jharkhand. 
• The recognition is based on the instrument's distinctive construction, traditional 
manufacturing methods, acoustic qualities and cultural association with Jharkhand. 
• Mandar is closely associated with the Oraon tribe and is widely used in folk music, dances, 
festivals and community life in the Chotanagpur region. 
 
MHA Identifies 28 Places and Geographical Features in Ladakh 
• In October 2026, the Ministry of Home Affairs, in consultation with the Ladakh administration, 
identified 28 places and geographical features for standardised naming on the official Survey 
of India map. 
• The listed features include peaks, mountains, land areas, glaciers, passes, a valley, a river, 
a lake and another water body. 
• Named features include Atisha Giri, Kanishka Peak, Karun Pir, Drak Karpo, Parpik Glacier 
and Skamri Glacier. 
• Other features include Chapchingal Pass, Shachmirk Pass, Yangpa River, Guru Rinpoche 
Lake, Shkorga Valley and a hot spring. 
• The initiative aims to standardise geographical references and improve public awareness of 
the names and locations of features in Ladakh.

Odisha Launches India's First Dedicated MICE Incentive Portal 
• The Odisha government launched India's first dedicated digital portal for Meetings, 
Incentives, Conferences and Exhibitions (MICE) tourism in October 2026. 
• Deputy Chief Minister and Tourism Minister Pravati Parida launched it during the 7th Odisha 
Travel Bazaar 2026 in Bhubaneswar. 
• The portal enables event organisers and tourism operators to apply online for state financial 
incentives and promotes Odisha as a destination for corporate events. 
• Under the Odisha Tourism (Amendment) Policy, 2026, eligible events held from April 1 to 
September 30 can receive ₹1,000 per room per day. 
• Eligibility requires a minimum booking of 100 rooms per day in three -star-or-above hotels, 
with at least 50% of occupants coming from outside Odisha. 
 
Uttar Pradesh to Deploy 1,725 Electric Buses in 18 Cities Under GCC Model 
The Uttar Pradesh Cabinet, chaired by Chief Minister Yogi Adityanath, approved the deployment 
of 1,725 air-conditioned electric buses across 18 cities to strengthen urban public transport. 
The buses will operate under the Gross Cost Contract (GCC) model, with private operators 
responsible for procurement, charging infrastructure, drivers, operation and maintenance. The 
contract period will be 12 years from the start of commercial operations. 
The state government will provide subsidies of ₹40 lakh per 12 -metre bus and ₹35 lakh per 9 -
metre bus. Municipal corporations and the Noida Authority will provide land free of cost for bus 
depots. 
Lucknow and Kanpur will receive 300 buses each, while Varanasi will receive 250 and Gorakhpur 
150. Other cities include Agra, Ghaziabad, Meerut, Noida and Ayodhya. 
The initiative aims to improve service quality, reduce the government's operational expenditure 
burden, promote electric mobility and generate local employment, with preference proposed for 
buses manufactured in Uttar Pradesh at the L-1 rate.

Delhi Cabinet approves ₹50 crore Go-Delhi Foundation to turn startup and innovator ideas 
into market-ready products 
Key Points 
The Delhi government has approved the Go -Delhi Foundation with a corpus of ₹50 crore to 
support startups, MSMEs and young innovators. The approval came in a recent Cabinet meeting 
chaired by Chief Minister Rekha Gupta. 
The foundation will provide product engineering, prototyping, laboratories and testing facilities, 
along with technical guidance, mentorship, incubation and investor connections. 
It will support entrepreneurs at every stage, from product development and testing to certification 
preparation and market access. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
116 
 
The foundation aims to promote startups, innovation and entrepreneurship in the capital and to 
bring existing resources onto one common platform. It is intended to create new enterprises, 
attract investment and generate employment. 
Delhi is home to nearly 21,000 startups recognised by the Department for Promotion of Industry 
and Internal Trade (DPIIT). 
The foundation will work through a hub-and-spoke model, delivering programmes through existing 
facilities and partner institutions across Delhi, and will also work with industry, educational 
institutions and investors. 
Government departments can bring their real -world challenges to the foundation, and solutions 
developed by startups can be tested through pilot projects and adopted if they succeed. 
The foundation will be a not-for-profit company under Section 8 of the Companies Act, 2013 and 
will function under the Department of Industries of the Delhi government. 
 
Uttar Pradesh approves two industrial enclaves worth ₹480 crore across 420 acres in 
Gorakhpur and Banda 
Key Points 
The Uttar Pradesh government has approved two industrial enclave projects worth a combined 
₹480 crore across more than 420 acres in Gorakhpur and Banda districts, to boost industrial 
infrastructure in eastern Uttar Pradesh. 
The Gorakhpur project is proposed by the Gorakhpur Industrial Development Authority (GIDA), 
and the Banda project by the Uttar Pradesh Expressways Industrial Development Authority 
(UPEIDA). 
Both were approved under the Bharat Audyogik Vikas Yojana (BHAVYA). Their detailed project 
reports (DPRs) will now go to the National Level Steering Committee. 
The Dhuriyapar Industrial Area in Gorakhpur will span more than 120 acres at an estimated cost 
of ₹102 crore. It will include industrial plots, flatted factories, warehousing and a Common Facility 
Centre. 
The Integrated Manufacturing and Logistics Cluster (IMLC), Phase II in Banda will cover 310 
acres at an estimated cost of ₹378 crore. It will include industrial, logistics, commercial, residential 
and social infrastructure, along with a sewage treatment plant (STP), a common effluent treatment 
plant (CETP) and a power substation. 
Once the facilities are ready, UPEIDA will offer industrial plots to investors. 
Chief Secretary S P Goyal has directed officials to speed up statutory approvals so that prescribed 
timelines are met. The projects are expected to attract investment, create employment and 
support ancillary industries. 
Separately, the state plans to launch fresh industrial projects worth ₹8 trillion at the proposed 
Ground Breaking Ceremony (GBC) later this year. Invest UP has listed projects worth ₹6 trillion, 
and the government expects the total to rise to ₹8 trillion before the event.

The Uttar Pradesh government has launched subsidised sugar sales at ₹52 per kg, compared 
with the prevailing market price of around ₹65 per kg, to provide relief to consumers. 
The initiative will begin in Lucknow at the Lal Bahadur Shastri Ganna Kisan Sansthan premises 
through the Sugar Corporation and Cooperative Sugar Mills Federation. 
The subsidised sale will operate on working days from 10 am to 5 pm, with plans to extend it to 
other cities if prices remain elevated.

absence of fresh sugar arrivals. 
The state government had also recently started selling onions at ₹30 per kg through mobile vans 
to control rising prices.

Centre Approves ₹27,370 Crore Power Transmission Project for Andhra Pradesh Under 
GEC-III 
The Central Government has approved a ₹27,370 crore power transmission project for Andhra 
Pradesh under the Green Energy Corridor Phase -III (GEC-III) scheme to strengthen the state's 
renewable energy transmission infrastructure. 
The project, proposed by the Transmission Corporation of Andhra Pradesh (APTRANSCO), 
involves constructing 7,872 circuit km of 400 kV and 220 kV transmission lines and adding 9,500 
MVA of substation capacity, including five new substations. 
The first phase of the project is expected to be commissioned by FY 2029-30. 
The Union Cabinet approved the overall GEC -III scheme on September 30, 2026, with a total 
outlay of ₹1.86 lakh crore to evacuate up to 135 GW of renewable energy through state 
transmission networks by FY 2032-33. 
The scheme includes around ₹1.36 lakh crore for intra -state transmission infrastructure and 
₹50,000 crore for 50 GWh of Battery Energy Storage Systems (BESS). The Centre will provide 
₹54,082 crore in financial support to offset intra-state transmission charges. 
 
Tamil Nadu Announces Five-Year Age Relaxation for Government Job Aspirants 
Tamil Nadu Chief Minister C. Joseph Vijay has announced a five -year age relaxation for 
candidates appearing in state government competitive examinations. The relaxation will remain 
effective for five years. 
The relaxation will be over and above the existing age relaxation provisions applicable to different 
social categories and candidates with disabilities. 
For Group I services, the upper age limit for general category candidates will increase from 32 to 
37 years, while for reserved category candidates, it will increase from 37 to 42 years. 
For differently-abled candidates in Group I, the revised age limits will be 47 years for the general 
category and 52 years for reserved categories. 
For Group II and II -A services, the upper age limit for general category candidates will increase 
from 30 to 35 years, while for differently -abled general category candidates, it will increase from 
40 to 45 years.

constituencies.

The Union Cabinet approved amendments to the LAHDC regulation on October 14, 2026. 
Each council can now have a minimum of 7 and a maximum of 24 seats, with provision for 
nominating four members from women and unrepresented communities to strengthen grassroots 
representation.

2026. 
The Bill proposes to establish the FNTA to promote decentralised governance and self -
governance in six eastern districts: Tuensang, Mon, Longleng, Kiphire, Noklak and Shamator. 
The authority will exercise legislative, executive, administrative and financial powers over 46 
subjects, including education, public health, agriculture, rural development and fisheries. 
The FNTA will have 62 members: 40 directly elected members, 20 elected MLAs from the six 
districts as ex -officio members without voting rights, and two members nominated by the 
Governor. 
One-third of the directly elected seats will be reserved for women. Its Executive Council will have 
up to nine members and be headed by a Chief Executive Member.

Maharashtra Approves Kolamarka and Expanded Tansa Wildlife Sanctuaries 
The Maharashtra State Board for Wildlife (SBWL), chaired by Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis, 
approved proposals to upgrade the Kolamarka Conservation Reserve and the expanded Tansa 
area to Wildlife Sanctuary status. 
The decisions were taken during the 26th meeting of the SBWL under the Wild Life (Protection) 
Act, 1972. 
The Kolamarka Conservation Reserve in Gadchiroli district is known for its population of the 
endangered Asiatic Wild Water Buffalo. 
The expanded Tansa Wildlife Sanctuary proposal includes 588.767 hectares of revenue and 
private land from six villages in the Thane–Palghar region. 
The board also approved 14 conservation reserves across seven districts: Pune, Raigad, Nashik, 
Nandurbar, Thane, Palghar and Chandrapur.

Tata Projects and MDS Aero sign MoU to develop aero-engine testing infrastructure 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
124 
 
• Tata Projects Limited and Canada's MDS Aero Support Corporation signed an MoU to 
develop advanced aero-engine testing infrastructure in India. 
• The partnership aims to strengthen India's aerospace and propulsion ecosystem through 
specialised gas-turbine engine testing facilities. 
• Tata Projects will lead engineering, procurement and construction activities, including civil, 
structural, mechanical, electrical, plumbing, HVAC, exhaust and cooling systems. 
• MDS Aero will provide expertise in the design, integration, commissioning and lifecycle 
support of gas-turbine engine test facilities. 
• The infrastructure will support engine performance evaluation, validation and certification. 
 
DIBD and CAG Launch CAG-BHASHINI Anuvaad 
• The Digital India BHASHINI Division (DIBD) and the Comptroller and Auditor General of India 
(CAG) launched CAG-BHASHINI Anuvaad in September 2026 during the closing ceremony 
of Hindi Diwas and Hindi Pakhwada 2026. 
• The AI-based platform translates official documents and audit reports using context -aware 
translation and consistent terminology. 
• It supports secure processing, data sovereignty and human oversight to improve translation 
quality and accuracy. 
• BHASHINI was launched in 2022 under the National Language Translation Mission. 
• According to the supplied report, BHASHINI powers over 800 government websites, has 
enabled more than 9 billion AI inferences and supports 36 Indian text languages, 23 Indian 
voice languages and 35 international languages. 
 
DBN Signs Agreement for Amended BharatNet Programme in Maharashtra 
• Digital Bharat Nidhi (DBN), under the Department of Telecommunications, signed an 
agreement with the Maharashtra government, MDIL, BSNL and MahaIT in October 2026. 
• The Government of India has approved ₹10,520 crore for implementing the Amended 
BharatNet Programme (ABP) in Maharashtra under the state-led model. 
• The programme will cover 28,237 Gram Panchayats: 15,171 Phase -I Gram Panchayats, 
12,740 Phase-II Gram Panchayats and 326 newly created Gram Panchayats. 
• Additionally, 15,799 villages will receive connectivity on demand. 
• DBN supports telecom access in rural areas and succeeded the Universal Service Obligation 
Fund (USOF), established on April 1, 2002, under the Telecommunications Act, 2023. 
 
SpaceX Launches 130 Payloads, Including Google AI Chips, into Low Earth Orbit 
• SpaceX launched 130 payloads aboard a Falcon 9 rocket from Vandenberg Space Force 
Base, California, as part of its Transporter-18 smallsat rideshare mission in September 2026. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
125 
 
• The payloads were deployed into their designated Low Earth Orbit (LEO). 
• The launch included Project Suncatcher, Google’s programme to explore the possibility of 
building an orbital data centre. 
• Its first in-orbit experiment, MVP, is designed to test how Google’s Tensor Processing Units 
(TPUs), or AI chips, function in space and support AI and machine-learning workloads. 
• Other payloads included CubeSats, microsatellites, hosted payloads, two spacecraft 
equipped with re -entry vehicles and four orbital transfer vehicles intended to deploy 41 
payloads later. 
 
QNu Labs and BISAG-N Demonstrate India's First 5.56-km Free-Space QKD Link 
• QNu Labs, BISAG -N and IIT Gandhinagar demonstrated India's first free -space Quantum 
Key Distribution (QKD) link over 5.56 km in September 2026. 
• The field trial was conducted on September 27 –28, 2026, establishing a secure quantum 
communication link between BISAG-N and IIT Gandhinagar. 
• A Pointing, Acquisition and Tracking (PAT) system maintained stable communication with a 
Quantum Bit Error Rate below 5%. 
• The system generated secure encryption keys at 230–260 bits per second. 
• The keys were integrated with the Vedic Kavach platform, combining hardware -based QKD 
using the Armos device with software -based post -quantum cryptography for end -to-end 
encryption and decryption. 
 
Scientists Discover New Lizard Species in Peru 
• Scientists associated with the Natural History Museum of San Marcos University in Peru 
discovered a new lizard species named Liolaemus arequipensis in the Arequipa region of 
southern Peru. 
• The species was discovered at an elevation of 4,818 metres above sea level, and the findings 
were published in the scientific journal Diversity. 
• It belongs to the Liolaemus genus, a diverse group of South American lizards commonly 
found in the Andes. 
• The lizard has a compact body measuring 6–8 cm. Females have square -shaped spots on 
their backs, while males have oblique spots and white scales that help camouflage them in 
rocky surroundings. 
• These lizards generally feed on plants and small arthropods, including spiders, beetles, true 
bugs and insect larvae.

Vayona Energy Unveils 5 MW Wind Turbine Platform in Chennai 
Vayona Energy unveiled its next -generation Vayona 5.0/193, a 5 MW onshore wind turbine 
platform, at Windergy India 2026 in Chennai. 
Developed in collaboration with Aerovide GmbH, Germany, the turbine features a 193-metre rotor 
and is designed to maximise energy generation in low and ultra-low wind conditions and operate 
reliably in high temperatures. 
The platform combines German engineering expertise with Indian manufacturing capabilities, 
local supply chains and lifecycle maintenance services. 
It incorporates digital twin technology and integration with Vayona’s Remote Operations Centre 
(ROC) for continuous performance monitoring, early fault detection and predictive maintenance. 
The platform expands Vayona’s presence in the 5 MW segment and is designed for standalone 
wind projects, wind-solar hybrid systems and renewable energy requirements of commercial and 
industrial customers.

Science and Technology. 
The websites support 22 Indian languages through BHASHINI integration and comply with the 
Digital Brand Identity Manual (DBIM) 3.0 and Guidelines for Indian Government Websites and 
Apps (GIGW) 3.0. 
Services: The portals provide single -window access to information on research grants, 
fellowships, schemes, calls for proposals, application guidelines, awards and notifications. 
Accessibility: Features include adjustable font sizes, high -contrast display, link highlighting, 
spacing adjustments and screen-reader compatibility. 
The websites use a modular content management system, SSL/TLS encryption, cybersecurity 
assessments aligned with applicable CERT-In guidelines and a content delivery network for faster 
access. 
They were developed jointly by DST, the National Informatics Centre (NIC), the project 
management team and the development agency.

Monthly Beepedia October 2026

Capabilities: The platform will follow an “ask, do and escalate” model, enabling it to answer

Tata Power Partners with Norway’s Ocean Sun for Floating Solar Technology 
In October 2026, Tata Power Company Limited partnered with Norway-based renewable energy 
technology company Ocean Sun to introduce membrane-based floating solar technology in India. 
The agreement was signed on 7 October 2026 during the second India–EFTA Prosperity Summit 
in New Delhi. 
Pilot Project: The partnership will develop a nearly 300-kilowatt-peak (kWp) floating solar pilot at 
Mulshi reservoir in Maharashtra, marking Ocean Sun’s first installation in India. 
Technology: Solar photovoltaic modules are mounted on a floating hydro -elastic membrane. 
Ocean Sun will provide the technology, while Tata Power will own the project assets. 
The pilot will assess energy generation, capacity utilisation factor (CUF), reliability, operations 
and maintenance costs, and commercial viability. 
According to the National Institute of Solar Energy (NISE), India has floating solar potential 
exceeding 102 GWp. 
Tata Power: Established in 1911 by Jamsetji Tata and headquartered in Mumbai, Maharashtra.

NITI Aayog’s Frontier Tech Hub released the Data Centre Evaluation Framework in October 2026 
to help states assess investment proposals and promote sustainable data-centre development. 
The framework focuses on five pillars: power and energy; water and cooling management; 
environmental protection; economic value creation; and policy, governance and implementation. 
India’s data-centre capacity increased from approximately 375 MW in 2020 to 1.5 GW in 2026, 
driven by growing AI and computing demand.

ISRO Validates Gaganyaan Module Separation Tests 
The Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO) successfully completed Crew Module and 
Service Module separation tests at the Vikram Sarabhai Space Centre in Thiruvananthapuram, 
Kerala, in October 2026. 
The tests validated the separation system and its ability to disconnect electrical and fluid-service 
connections between the two modules. 
The tests support preparations for Gaganyaan -1, the first uncrewed mission, planned for 2026 
and carrying the Vyommitra half-humanoid. 
India’s Gaganyaan human spaceflight mission aims to send a three -member crew to low Earth 
orbit at an altitude of approximately 400 km for a three-day mission, followed by safe recovery in 
the Indian Ocean.

The Technology Development Board (TDB), under the Department of Science and Technology, 
signed agreements with ideaForge and QNu Labs for research, development and innovation 
support for indigenous drone logistics and quantum-secure technologies. 
The TDB–ideaForge project involves developing YETI, a heavy -lift autonomous aerial logistics 
vehicle, with a project cost of ₹302 crore, including ₹151 crore in debt financing support. 
YETI is designed to carry a 200 -kg payload over 200 km and operate at altitudes of up to 6,500 
metres. 
The TDB–QNu Labs project, costing ₹300 crore with ₹150 crore in optionally convertible debt 
support, will develop India’s Quantum Secure and Sensing Networks, focusing on identity, 
location integrity, network security and time synchronisation.
