Three New Centipede Species Discovered in the Western Ghats 
Researchers from the Zoological Survey of India’s (ZSI) Western Ghats Regional Centre and 
Malabar Christian College discovered three new centipede species in Kerala and Karnataka. 
The species are Rhysida quadrispinosa, Rhysida coorgensis and Rhysida neyyarensis. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
132 
 
Their findings, published in the International Journal of Tropical Insect Science, increased the 
number of known Rhysida species in India to 23. 
The species were named after distinctive physical features and locations, including Coorg in 
Karnataka and Neyyar Wildlife Sanctuary in Kerala. 
 
IIT Kharagpur to Establish India's First Dedicated School of Physical AI 
IIT Kharagpur will establish India's first dedicated academic school focused on Physical Artificial 
Intelligence (AI) with an investment of ₹35 crore. It will be named the Benjamin Gupta School of 
Physical AI. 
The school will be established through an MoU signed between IIT Kharagpur, its alumnus and 
philanthropist Vinod Gupta, and the IITKGP Foundation USA. 
Funding includes ₹12.5 crore from the Vinod Gupta family, ₹10 crore from an anonymous 
alumnus, and the remaining amount from the institute. 
The school will focus on intelligent robotics, autonomous systems, scientific AI, digital twins, 
physics-informed machine learning and advanced sensing. 
Unlike conventional AI, Physical AI focuses on developing intelligent systems that can understand 
and interact with the physical world using real -world sensing, scientific principles and intelligent 
control. Academic programmes are expected to begin in the  upcoming session, subject to 
necessary approvals. 
 
PhonePe Rebrands Indus Appstore as PhonePe Appstore, Expands Digital Marketplace 
Walmart-owned fintech company PhonePe has rebranded its homegrown app marketplace, Indus 
Appstore, as PhonePe Appstore to strengthen its presence in India's digital marketplace. 
The platform is currently available on more than 200 million devices through partnerships with 
Original Equipment Manufacturers (OEMs). 
It offers applications and games, including ChatGPT, Duolingo, ShareChat and Sony LIV, and is 
expanding its services to include gift cards, digital top-ups and other digital products. 
PhonePe is also launching phonepeappstore.com, a webstore that will allow users to discover 
and purchase digital goods through desktop and mobile browsers without downloading a 
dedicated app. 
The expansion aims to provide developers with additional monetisation opportunities through 
advertisements, gift cards and top-ups, while leveraging PhonePe's payment infrastructure. 
 
BHASHINI and IISc Develop AI-Based Text-to-Speech Models for Sourashtra 
The Digital India BHASHINI Division, under the Ministry of Electronics and Information 
Technology (MeitY), in collaboration with the Indian Institute of Science (IISc), Bengaluru, 
developed Text-to-Speech (TTS) models for the Sourashtra language. 
Sourashtra is a low -resource Indo -Aryan language spoken predominantly by the Sourashtra 
community in Tamil Nadu. 
Four models based on the Variational Inference with adversarial learning for end -to-end Text-to-
Speech (VITS) architecture have been developed. 
The models generate speech at 22,050 Hz and support male and female voices in Tamil and 
Sourashtra scripts. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
133 
 
The corpus, developed by IISc Bengaluru's SPIRE Lab, contains over 28 hours of recordings and 
is available on BHASHINI's AIKosh platform to support language preservation and digital 
inclusion. 
 
C-DOT and IIT (BHU) Sign MoU to Establish Sixth Centre of Excellence for 6G and Quantum 
Technologies 
The Centre for Development of Telematics (C -DOT), under the Department of 
Telecommunications (DoT), signed an MoU with IIT (BHU), Varanasi, to establish its sixth Centre 
of Excellence (CoE). 
The centre aims to strengthen India's indigenous telecommunications research and development 
ecosystem by combining C-DOT's expertise with IIT (BHU)'s academic capabilities. 
Its focus areas include 5G Advanced/6G technologies, Artificial Intelligence, quantum 
technologies, cybersecurity, wireless communications, large-scale Multiple-Input Multiple-Output 
(MIMO) and Integrated Sensing and Communication. 
The MoU was signed in the presence of Dr Pankaj Kumar Dalela, Executive Vice President of C-
DOT, and Professor Amit Patra, Director of IIT (BHU). 
C-DOT's other five CoEs are located at IIT Kanpur, IIT Gandhinagar, IIT Roorkee, IIT Hyderabad 
and IISc Bengaluru through its Foundation for Science, Innovation and Development (FSID). 
 
C-DAC and Bank of India Sign MoU to Explore Quantum Technologies for Banking 
Innovation 
The Centre for Development of Advanced Computing (C-DAC), under the Ministry of Electronics 
and Information Technology (MeitY), signed an MoU with Bank of India (BoI) to explore emerging 
technologies for banking and financial services. 
The partnership will combine expertise in advanced computing, quantum technologies, Artificial 
Intelligence, blockchain and cybersecurity through joint research, pilot projects, technology 
demonstrations and training. 
The organisations will develop a 'Quantum Ecosystem for Financial Innovation' to explore 
quantum-inspired and quantum-safe technologies for improving banking security and resilience. 
Key applications include AI -based document processing, customer support, fraud detection, 
credit assessment, financial analytics, smart contracts and digital identity. 
The collaboration will also involve developing Proofs of Concept (PoCs) in secure sandbox 
environments and assessing their technical feasibility before wider adoption. 
 
New Eyeless Freshwater Cave Snail Species Discovered in China's Yunnan Province 
Scientists discovered a new freshwater cave snail species named Erhaia dijiang in a high-altitude 
mountain cave in Yuxi City, Yunnan Province, China, at an elevation of 2,062 metres. 
The discovery was published in the journal Subterranean Biology. Only three living adult 
specimens were found, raising concerns about its conservation. 
The species has lost its eyes and body pigmentation and possesses a nearly transparent, cone -
shaped shell with long, slender tentacles as adaptations to underground life. 
It was named after Di Jiang, a faceless mythical creature described in the ancient Chinese text 
Classic of Mountains and Seas (Shan Hai Jing). 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
134 
 
The snail inhabits shallow cave waters less than one metre deep, among submerged gravel and 
broken rocks, at a water temperature of approximately 20°C. 
 
Jitendra Singh Inaugurates World's First Atmospheric Chemistry and Aerosol Toxicity 
Analysis Laboratory 
Union Minister of State (Independent Charge) Dr Jitendra Singh inaugurated the Atmospheric 
Chemistry and Aerosol Toxicity Analysis Laboratory (AATAL) at the Indian Institute of Tropical 
Meteorology (IITM), New Delhi. 
It is described as the world's first integrated facility of its kind and has been established under 
Mission Mausam, launched in 2025 by the Ministry of Earth Sciences. 
The laboratory analyses atmospheric gases, aerosols, PM2.5, metals and toxic elements to 
identify sources of air pollution in the Delhi-NCR region. 
It helps distinguish pollution arising from vehicles, industries, biomass and crop -residue burning, 
construction activities, road dust and pollution transported from neighbouring regions. 
AATAL is integrated with IITM's AIRWISE (Air Quality Warning and Integrated Decision Support 
System for Emissions), which provides air -quality forecasts at a spatial resolution of 400 metres 
for the next 72 hours. 
 
LTTS and Nokia Sign Five-Year Engineering and R&D Agreement 
L&T Technology Services Limited (LTTS) signed a five-year agreement with Nokia to establish a 
dedicated Offshore Development Centre (ODC) in India. 
The centre will support Nokia's global engineering, research and development operations and will 
integrate LTTS capabilities with Nokia's global R&D teams. 
The agreement aligns with LTTS's Lakshya 31 strategy. 
Key areas include research, product engineering, embedded systems, software development, 
system integration and ongoing product support. 
The centre will focus on next -generation telecommunications, AI infrastructure, 5G networks, 
intelligent automation and advanced computing. Nokia is headquartered in Espoo, Finland, and 
was established in 1865. 
 
New Flowering Plant Species 'Osbeckia assamensis' Discovered in Assam 
Researchers Namita Nath of Gauhati University and Barnali Das of Pragjyotish College 
documented a new flowering plant species, Osbeckia assamensis, in Manas National Park, 
Assam. 
The discovery was based on floristic surveys conducted between 2021 and 2025 and published 
in the international journal Phytotaxa. 
The species belongs to the family Melastomataceae and genus Osbeckia. 
It is a perennial shrub growing 2 –2.5 metres tall, with large opposite leaves and pink flowers 
measuring 3–3.5 cm. 
It bears 30–35 flowers per inflorescence, compared with 5–12 flowers in Osbeckia nayarii, helping 
distinguish the newly documented species. 
 
Two New Flowering Plant Species Discovered in Arunachal Pradesh 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia October 2026 
135 
 
Researchers from the Botanical Survey of India (BSI) and Madras Christian College discovered 
two new flowering plant species, Ophiorrhiza shidiensis and Ophiorrhiza pemae, in the Vijoynagar 
region of Changlang district, Arunachal Pradesh. 
Both species belong to the Rubiaceae family, commonly known as the coffee family. 
Their findings were published in the scientific journal Taiwania on October 23, 2026. 
Ophiorrhiza shidiensis is an erect perennial herb that grows up to one metre tall. Its name comes 
from Shidi, the traditional name for Gandhigram used by the Lisu (Yobin) community. 
Ophiorrhiza pemae grows approximately 25–40 cm tall and was named in honour of Arunachal 
Pradesh Chief Minister Pema Khandu. 
 
New Microscopic Land Snail Species Discovered in Maharashtra 
Researchers from the Thackeray Wildlife Foundation discovered a new microscopic land snail 
species named Dicharax tilariensis in the Tilari region of Kolhapur district, Maharashtra. 
The species is currently known only from the Tilari region, making it endemic to the area. 
The research was published in the Journal of Conchology. 
The snail inhabits forest litter, decaying organic matter and moist soil in the semi -evergreen 
forests of Tilari. 
Specimens were collected near Swapnavel Point, close to Tilarinagar, at an elevation of 700–800 
metres. 
The research team included Amrut Bhosale, Tejas Thackeray and Akshay Khandekar.
